# AskRef volunteer referee scenario review pack

278 scenarios; 672 question phrasings.

Sources: PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); adopted IFAB fallback edition 2025/26.

These are editorial practice drafts linked to the bundled rule summaries. They have not been approved by PDSA or its Referee Coordinator. Review against the original adopted documents before match use. Suggested wording is communication advice, not an additional rule.

## 1. How long is a quarter?

Coach · U6 · Time · ID: u6-duration

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How many minutes do we play?”

U6 plays four 8-minute quarters. Quarter breaks are 2 minutes and halftime is 5 minutes. Confirm any roster or weather changes with the coordinator.

**You can say:** “We play eight-minute quarters in U6.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 2. How long is a quarter?

Coach · U8 · Time · ID: u8-duration

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How many minutes do we play?”
- Also asked as: “How long is a quater?”

U8 plays four 10-minute quarters. Quarter breaks are 2 minutes and halftime is 5 minutes. Confirm any roster or weather changes with the coordinator.

**You can say:** “We play ten-minute quarters in U8.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 3. How long is a half?

Coach · U10 · Time · ID: u10-duration

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How many minutes do we play?”

U10 plays two 25-minute halves with a 5-minute halftime, subject to approved roster or weather changes.

**You can say:** “We play twenty-five-minute halves in U10.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 4. How long is a half?

Coach · U12/U14/U18 · Time · ID: older-duration

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How many minutes do we play?”

U12, U14 and U18 play two 30-minute halves with a 5-minute halftime, subject to approved roster or weather changes.

**You can say:** “This division plays thirty-minute halves.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 5. Are you adding time for substitutions?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Time · ID: added-time

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can we get stoppage time?”

PDSA adds no time for ordinary stoppages.

**You can say:** “Our local rules do not add time for ordinary stoppages.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 6. Can we have a water break?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Time · ID: water-break

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “We have no subs and it is really hot. Can we take a break?”

For U10–U18, teams without substitutes or playing in extreme heat or wind may agree with the center referee on a 2-minute mid-half water break. The Board makes the final decision game by game; confirm the arrangement.

**You can say:** “Let us confirm the approved water-break arrangement with the coordinator.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 7. Can we wait for our missing players?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Time · ID: late-team

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “The other team is late. How long do we wait?”

A team short of players may receive a delay of up to 10 minutes. That delay comes out of playing time. Ask the coordinator if the team still cannot start; do not invent a forfeit procedure.

**You can say:** “The rules allow up to ten minutes, taken out of playing time. I will check with the coordinator if we still cannot start.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 8. What size ball do we use?

Player · U6/U8 · Equipment · ID: ball-small

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Is this the right ball size?”

PDSA U6 and U8 use a size 3 ball.

**You can say:** “We use a size three ball in this division.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 2 – The Ball, page 7 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 9. What size ball do we use?

Player · U10 · Equipment · ID: ball-u10

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Is this the right ball size?”

PDSA U10 uses a size 4 ball.

**You can say:** “U10 uses a size four ball.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 2 – The Ball, page 7 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 10. What size ball do we use?

Player · U12/U14/U18 · Equipment · ID: ball-older

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Is this the right ball size?”

PDSA U12, U14 and U18 use a size 5 ball.

**You can say:** “We use a size five ball in this division.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 2 – The Ball, page 7 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 11. Can my child play without shin guards?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Equipment · ID: shin-guards

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Do shin guards go over the socks?”

Shin guards of suitable size and material are required and must be covered by socks.

**You can say:** “Shin guards need to be on and covered by socks before playing.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 4 – The Players’ Equipment, page 9 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 12. Can we just tape the earrings?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Equipment · ID: earrings

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “My child just got ears pierced. Can the earrings stay in?”

PDSA Law 4 allows small stud earrings covered with tape or equivalent; other jewelry exceptions require Referee Coordinator or Board approval. Confirm ambiguous equipment or safety concerns with the coordinator.

**You can say:** “PDSA allows covered small studs. Let me confirm any other jewelry or safety concern with the coordinator.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 4 – The Players’ Equipment, page 9 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 13. Can my child play with a cast?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Equipment · ID: cast

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Is a padded brace allowed?”

Hard casts or braces must be approved and padded. The summary does not identify the approval procedure; confirm it with the coordinator.

**You can say:** “We need to confirm approval and padding before play.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 4 – The Players’ Equipment, page 9 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 14. Our keeper has the same shirt as the team. Is that okay?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Equipment · ID: keeper-colors

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Does the goalie need a different color?”

Teams and goalkeepers must wear distinguishable colors. Use a goalkeeper pinnie if needed.

**You can say:** “Please put a distinguishable pinnie on the goalkeeper.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 4 – The Players’ Equipment, page 9 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 15. Can I play in boots?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Equipment · ID: boots

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Are toe cleats allowed?”

The bundled PDSA rule summary requires closed-toe footwear and prohibits toe-cleats or boots of any type.

**You can say:** “We need footwear that follows the PDSA equipment rule.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 4 – The Players’ Equipment, page 9 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 16. Does my child have to play half the game?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Players · ID: playing-time

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How much playing time does every child get?”

PDSA requires each player to play at least 50% of the game. Raise playing-time concerns with the coach or coordinator; this excerpt does not specify an in-game penalty.

**You can say:** “The local rule is at least half the game for each player. The coordinator can help with a concern.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 17. Can I sub while the ball is moving?

Coach · U6 · Players · ID: u6-subs

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can we substitute during play?”

PDSA U6 allows substitutions anytime. Do not apply this exception to older divisions.

**You can say:** “U6 allows substitutions during play.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 18. Can I sub while the ball is moving?

Coach · U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Players · ID: older-subs

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can we substitute during play?”

Above U6, substitutions are during stoppages under the listed PDSA procedure. This summary does not include every permission detail; consult the full rule or coordinator.

**You can say:** “Please wait for a stoppage and follow the substitution procedure.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 19. Can another player go in goal?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Players · ID: keeper-change

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can we swap goalkeepers?”

A player may become goalkeeper if the referee is informed and the change takes place during a stoppage.

**You can say:** “Tell me about the change and make it during a stoppage.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 20. Can I go on the field to help?

Coach · U6 · Players · ID: u6-coach

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can a coach stay on the field?”

PDSA permits one U6 coach on the field throughout the game.

**You can say:** “One U6 coach may be on the field.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 1 – The Field of Play, page 7 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 21. Is that offside?

Parent · U6/U8 · Offside · ID: small-offside

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Why are you not calling offside?”
- Also asked as: “Is offside called?”

PDSA does not call offside in U6 or U8. Do not award an offside free kick in those divisions.

**You can say:** “We do not call offside in this division under PDSA rules.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 11 – Offside, page 14 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 22. Is offside called?

Parent · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Offside · ID: older-offside

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Do we use offside in this division?”

Offside applies in U10, U12, U14 and U18. A specific offside decision needs the IFAB conditions and facts of the play; position alone is not enough information to decide here.

**You can say:** “Offside applies here. For a particular play, I need to consider what happened.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 11 – Offside, page 14 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 23. The ball touched the line. Is it out?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Ball out · ID: ball-line

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Does the whole ball have to go out?”

The whole ball must pass over the touchline or goal line, on the ground or in the air. Touching the line alone does not make it out.

**You can say:** “The whole ball has to cross the line.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 9 – The Ball In and Out of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 24. Only half the ball crossed the goal line. Is it a goal?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Goals · ID: whole-goal

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Does the whole ball need to cross for a goal?”

For a goal, the whole ball must pass over the goal line. Half the ball crossing is not enough.

**You can say:** “The whole ball must cross for a goal.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 25. A defender touched the ball inside the goal box. What now?

Coach · U6/U8 · Goals · ID: small-goalbox-defender

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Their defender kicked it inside the goal box. Is that a goal?”

In PDSA U6/U8, a defender touching the ball inside the goal box results in a goal awarded to the attacking team.

**You can say:** “In this division, a defender touching it inside the goal box means a goal to the attacking team.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 26. An attacker touched the ball inside the goal box. What now?

Player · U6/U8 · Goals · ID: small-goalbox-attacker

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “I kicked it in their goal box. Does it count?”

In PDSA U6/U8, if an attacker touches the ball inside the goal box, award a goal kick to the defending team.

**You can say:** “In this division that is a goal kick, because an attacker touched it inside the goal box.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 27. The ball stopped inside the goal box. What now?

Player · U6/U8 · Goals · ID: small-goalbox-stopped

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Nobody touched it and it stopped in the goal box.”

In PDSA U6/U8, a ball stopping inside the goal box results in a goal kick to the defending team.

**You can say:** “The ball stopped in the goal box, so it is a goal kick.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 28. Can I run through the goal box without touching the ball?

Player · U6/U8 · Goals · ID: small-goalbox-run

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Am I allowed to step in the goal box?”

PDSA U6/U8 players may move through the goal box. They may not touch the ball inside it.

**You can say:** “You may run through it; do not touch the ball inside it.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 29. We are eight goals ahead. Is the game over?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Goals · ID: mercy

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “When does the mercy rule apply?”

The PDSA mercy rule applies when more than half the match is complete and the lead is more than eight goals. Exactly eight goals or exactly half the match does not meet those stated thresholds. The match then becomes a scrimmage with an official.

**You can say:** “Both conditions are needed: more than half played and a lead greater than eight.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 30. Can I score straight from kickoff?

Player · U6/U8 · Restarts · ID: small-kickoff-goal

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “The kickoff went straight into their goal. Does it count?”

PDSA U6/U8 does not allow a goal directly from kick-off. If it goes directly into the opponents’ goal, award a goal kick.

**You can say:** “In this division a goal cannot come straight from kick-off. Goal kick.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 8 – The Start and Restart of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 31. We won the coin toss. What can we choose?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: kickoff-choice

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “What does the rock paper scissors winner choose?”

The winner chooses the goal to attack or to take kick-off; the other team takes the remaining option. The team choosing the end takes second-half kick-off, and teams change ends.

**You can say:** “You can choose the end or the kick-off. The other team gets the remaining option.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 8 – The Start and Restart of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 32. Where do we stand for kickoff?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: kickoff-position

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can I stand in the other half at kickoff?”

At kick-off, all players except the kicker must be in their own half. Opponents stay outside the center circle until the ball is in play.

**You can say:** “Everyone except the kicker in your own half; opponents outside the circle.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 8 – The Start and Restart of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 33. Is that a penalty in this division?

Coach · U6/U8 · Fouls · ID: small-foul

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can you give a direct free kick for a foul?”

PDSA U6/U8 fouls result in indirect free kicks. Explain the offence to the player; no cards are awarded. Exact placement depends on where the offence happened, including the local near-goal-box rule.

**You can say:** “In this division fouls are indirect free kicks. I will explain what happened.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 34. Why did you not give a yellow card?

Parent · U6/U8 · Fouls · ID: small-cards

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Are there red cards in this division?”

PDSA U6/U8 awards no cards and requires the offence to be explained to the player. The excerpt does not establish a separate procedure for dangerous or persistent behavior; involve the coordinator when needed.

**You can say:** “We explain fouls without cards in this division. I can get coordinator help if needed.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 35. A player deliberately headed the ball. What is the restart?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Fouls · ID: heading

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Are intentional headers allowed?”

PDSA prohibits headers. Intentional heading results in an indirect free kick. Confirm the location and applicable placement exceptions before restarting.

**You can say:** “Intentional heading is not allowed here. It is an indirect free kick.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 36. The keeper held the ball longer than eight seconds. What is the restart?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Fouls · ID: keeper-eight

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Is holding the ball over eight seconds an indirect free kick?”

The bundled PDSA rule says goalkeeper hand/arm control for more than eight seconds is penalized with a corner kick. Consult the adopted IFAB procedure for the count and which corner; the summary does not give those details.

**You can say:** “The local rule specifies a corner kick for control beyond eight seconds. I need the full procedure for the count and corner.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 37. Can our keeper pick up my deliberate pass back?

Player · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Fouls · ID: backpass

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can the keeper handle a throw in from our own team?”

The PDSA summary lists handling a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in as goalkeeper handball offences. Check IFAB for precise conditions, exceptions and the restart; this short summary does not establish all of them.

**You can say:** “There are restrictions on handling a teammate pass or throw-in. The exact play matters.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 38. How far back does the wall go?

Player · U6/U8 · Restarts · ID: wall-small

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How many yards away for a free kick?”

PDSA U6/U8 defensive walls must be 4 yards away.

**You can say:** “Please move back four yards.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 39. How far back does the wall go?

Player · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: wall-older

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How many yards away for a free kick?”

PDSA U10 and older defensive walls must be 8 yards away.

**You can say:** “Please move back eight yards under our local rule.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 40. Where is a free kick near the goal box taken?

Player · U6/U8 · Restarts · ID: small-free-kick-box

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Do I take this free kick on the goal box line?”

For PDSA U6/U8 free kicks near the goal box, place the ball 4 yards directly away from the goal-box line.

**You can say:** “We move the ball four yards directly away from the goal-box line.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 41. An indirect free kick went straight into their goal. Does it count?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: indirect-direct-goal

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Nobody else touched the indirect kick before it went in their goal.”

If an indirect free kick goes directly into the opponents’ goal, award a goal kick. A goal needs a touch by another player.

**You can say:** “That was indirect and nobody else touched it, so it is a goal kick.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 42. Our free kick went straight into our own goal. Is it an own goal?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: free-kick-own-goal

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can a free kick directly score against our own team?”

A direct or indirect free kick going directly into the kicker’s own goal results in a corner kick.

**You can say:** “A free kick directly into your own goal gives the opponents a corner.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 43. Where do I put the ball for a penalty?

Player · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: penalty-placement

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How far from goal is the penalty spot?”

Place the ball stationary on the penalty mark. The bundled PDSA summary does not specify a distance from the goal line; do not guess a measurement.

**You can say:** “Use the marked penalty spot. I will not guess a distance from this summary.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 14 – The Penalty Kick, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 44. Where do the other players stand for a penalty?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: penalty-players

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can players stand next to the penalty taker?”

PDSA says other players must be at least 10 yards from the mark, behind the mark, inside the field and outside the penalty area.

**You can say:** “Everyone else: behind the mark, outside the penalty area, inside the field and ten yards away.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 14 – The Penalty Kick, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 45. Do we throw it or kick it in?

Player · U6 · Throw-ins · ID: u6-kickin

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Do we take throw ins in this division?”

PDSA U6 uses kick-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline, play it onto the field, and wait for another player’s touch before touching it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts the coach should choose another kicker.

**You can say:** “Ball on or outside the sideline. Kick it in, then wait for another player’s touch.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 46. Can we have a redo on that throw?

Coach · U8 · Throw-ins · ID: u8-throw-redo

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Does an illegal throw get another try?”

PDSA U8 receives a mulligan to retake an illegal throw-in throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and referee coordinator agree otherwise. Check whether an agreed exception applies.

**You can say:** “U8 gets a throw-in retry unless the approved local arrangement says otherwise.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 47. Can we have a redo on that throw?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: older-throw-redo

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Does an illegal throw get another try?”

PDSA removed throw-in mulligans for U10–U18 in the 2025/2026 issue. Use the adopted IFAB procedure to decide a particular infringement and restart.

**You can say:** “This division does not have the U8 throw-in retry.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 48. How do I take a throw in?

Player · U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: throw-technique

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can I lift both feet when I throw?”

Face the field. Keep part of each foot on the touchline or ground outside it. Use both hands, bringing the ball from behind and over your head, at the point where it left the field.

**You can say:** “Both hands, behind and over your head, and part of each foot on the line or ground outside it.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 49. The throw went straight into the goal. Does it count?

Parent · U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: throw-goal

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can you score straight from a throw in?”

A goal cannot be scored directly from a throw-in. Which goal it entered matters for the restart; check the adopted IFAB Law 15 before deciding that restart.

**You can say:** “A throw-in cannot score directly. Which goal did it enter?”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 50. Where do I take a goal kick?

Player · U6/U8 · Restarts · ID: small-goalkick

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Do I put a goal kick inside the goal box?”

For PDSA U6/U8, put the ball anywhere on the goal line, not in the goal box. Opponents remain in their own half until the ball is in play.

**You can say:** “On the goal line, outside the goal box. Opponents back in your own half.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 51. Does a goal kick have to leave the penalty area?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: goalkick-area

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can someone touch the goal kick inside the penalty area?”

The PDSA summary says the ball does not have to leave the penalty area before being touched by either team. This does not remove other IFAB requirements on opponents or when the ball is in play.

**You can say:** “It does not have to leave the penalty area. Other goal-kick conditions still apply.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 52. Which corner do I take it from?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: corner-placement

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can I move the corner flag out of the way?”

For a corner awarded because the ball crossed the goal line, use the corner area nearest the crossing point. Do not move the flag or have the referee hold it.

**You can say:** “Use the nearest corner area and leave the flag in place.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 17 – The Corner Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 53. Can you watch my phone video and change the call?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Sideline · ID: video

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “I recorded it. Will you review the replay?”

PDSA does not use video assistance or other videography to make match decisions.

**You can say:** “Our local rules do not use phone video to make match decisions.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 6 – The Other Match Officials, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 54. Who do I talk to about a referee complaint?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Sideline · ID: complaint

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can I complain to the referee about the result?”

PDSA directs performance or outcome concerns to the Referee Coordinator rather than the match official.

**You can say:** “Please bring that concern to the Referee Coordinator.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 55. Can the result change after the score card is signed?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Sideline · ID: signed-score

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “We signed the card but disagree with a goal.”

The PDSA summary says decisions about facts of play, goals and results are final once the match score card is signed. Direct concerns to the Referee Coordinator.

**You can say:** “The signed score card makes those decisions final under our local rule. Please raise concerns with the coordinator.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 56. We stopped for weather after half the game. Does it count?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Weather · ID: weather-complete

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Does a weather suspension count as a completed game?”

A weather suspension after at least 50% is complete counts as a completed match unless coaches and commissioner agree otherwise. Before 50%, reschedule and resume at the suspension point unless otherwise agreed. Confirm the completion point and any agreement.

**You can say:** “The halfway point matters. The commissioner can confirm whether it counts or resumes.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Appendix B – Weather and Field Condition Policy, page 21 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 57. The lightning alarm is sounding. Can we keep playing?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Weather · ID: lightning

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Do we stop when the field lightning alarm goes off?”

An alarming field lightning-detection alarm is a PDSA weather-suspension condition. Follow the field officials’ suspension directions. The bundled summary does not specify a return-to-play waiting period; do not invent one.

**You can say:** “The alarm is a suspension condition. Follow the field officials; I cannot guess when it is safe to resume.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Appendix B – Weather and Field Condition Policy, page 21 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 58. Is tournament overtime sudden death?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Tournament · ID: tournament-tie

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Does the first goal end overtime?”

For tied tournament matches, PDSA uses two consecutive five-minute overtime halves. Complete both halves regardless of the first-half overtime score. If still tied, use a penalty shootout.

**You can say:** “Both five-minute overtime halves are completed; the first goal does not end overtime.”

**Supporting sources:** Appendix A – Match Overtime, pages 19–20 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 59. It hit their hand. Is that always handball?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Fouls · ID: handball-question

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Everyone yelled handball. Do I have to call it?”

Do not decide from the shout alone. Ask what happened to the arm and ball, where contact occurred and which division is playing. The bundled PDSA summary refers foul definitions to IFAB and does not establish the full handball test.

**You can say:** “I need to judge the actual contact using the rule, not just the shout.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 60. My player fell over. Why was there no foul?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Fouls · ID: player-fell

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Does falling down mean a free kick?”

A fall alone is not enough information to identify an offence. Ask what the other player did and where it happened. Consult the adopted IFAB foul definitions; the bundled PDSA summary does not include the full test.

**You can say:** “Tell me what contact you mean. I have to judge the action under the rule.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 61. I got the ball first. How can it be a foul?

Player · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Fouls · ID: ball-first

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “I touched the ball before the player. Is that allowed?”

Do not decide solely from who touched the ball first. The complete challenge and its circumstances need the adopted IFAB foul definitions, which are not supplied in this PDSA summary.

**You can say:** “I need to consider the whole challenge, not only the first touch.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 62. Our player was standing behind their defenders. Is that offside?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Offside · ID: offside-specific

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “They were past the last defender. Why no flag?”

The PDSA summary confirms offside applies but refers specific rulings to IFAB or the coordinator. Ask where the player and ball were when a teammate played it and how the player became involved; do not decide from this description alone.

**You can say:** “I need the position and involvement at the relevant moment before deciding.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 11 – Offside, page 14 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 63. The ball hit the referee. Do we always stop?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Ball out · ID: ref-ball-contact

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “It bounced off you. Is it a dropped ball?”

The PDSA summary calls for a dropped ball when referee contact leaves the ball on the field and leads to a promising attack, a goal or a change of possession. Ask whether one of those occurred. Check adopted IFAB Law 8 for the recipient and placement; do not guess.

**You can say:** “What happened after it touched me matters. The full dropped-ball procedure also needs checking.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 8 – The Start and Restart of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 9 – The Ball In and Out of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 64. Is a second yellow a send off?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Fouls · ID: second-yellow

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can a sent off player stay in the game?”

The PDSA summary says a red card or second yellow sends a player or coach off and brings a one-match suspension, subject to review. It does not give every dismissal or replacement procedure.

**You can say:** “A second yellow is a send-off. The coordinator handles the review and further procedure.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 65. Do we use a sin bin?

Coach · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Fouls · ID: sin-bin

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can the player sit out a few minutes instead of a card?”

PDSA does not adopt IFAB temporary dismissals. Do not invent a temporary-dismissal option.

**You can say:** “Our local rules do not use temporary dismissals.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 66. The coach keeps arguing with me. What can I say?

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Sideline · ID: pressure

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Parents are shouting that I am wrong. What do I do?”

The PDSA summary directs performance and result concerns to the Referee Coordinator. Use a short calm explanation and get coordinator or experienced-referee support. This summary does not establish spectator sanctions; do not invent them.

**You can say:** “Please bring concerns to the Referee Coordinator. I am getting support.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 67. A player is hurt. How do we restart?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Fouls · ID: injury-procedure

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Do we drop the ball after an injury?”

The restart depends on why play stopped and whether the ball was already out. The bundled summary does not include the complete injury-stoppage or dropped-ball procedure. Get an experienced referee or coordinator and consult the adopted IFAB rules.

**You can say:** “I need to confirm why play stopped before choosing the restart.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 8 – The Start and Restart of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 68. Can I throw it in to myself?

Player · U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: throw-double-touch

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can I touch my throw before anyone else does?”

After taking a throw-in, the thrower must not touch the ball again until another player touches it. The PDSA summary does not give the complete infringement restart procedure.

**You can say:** “Wait for another player to touch it before you play it again.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 69. Can I kick a penalty backwards?

Player · U10/U12/U14/U18 · Restarts · ID: penalty-forward

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Can I touch my penalty twice before anyone else?”

PDSA requires the penalty to move forward. The kicker cannot touch it again until another player touches it. Check the complete rule for an infringement restart.

**You can say:** “Kick it forward, then wait for another player to touch it before playing it again.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 14 – The Penalty Kick, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 70. Where do throw-ins get placed?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: throw-placement-explainer

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Where do I take a throw in?”
- Also asked as: “Where is a throw-in taken?”
- Also asked as: “Where do you stand for a throw in?”
- Also asked as: “Where do throw ins get place?”

For divisions using throw-ins, take the throw-in at the spot where the ball crossed the touchline (sideline). Face the field, with part of each foot on the line or on the ground outside it, and throw with both hands from behind and over the head. U6 uses kick-ins instead.

**You can say:** “Back to where the ball went out. Both feet at the sideline, both hands behind and over your head.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 71. What divisions get mullegans for throw-ins?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: throw-mulligan-explainer

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Which divisions get mulligans for throw-ins?”
- Also asked as: “Who gets a redo on a throw in?”
- Also asked as: “Do we get a second try on throw-ins?”
- Also asked as: “Are throw in retakes allowed?”
- Also asked as: “Does U8 get throw-in mulligans?”

U8 gets a teaching retake for an illegal throw-in throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and Referee Coordinator agree otherwise. U6 uses kick-ins. U10, U12, U14 and U18 have no throw-in mulligans in this PDSA issue.

**You can say:** “U8 has the teaching retake; the older divisions do not. U6 uses kick-ins.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 72. How do I explain off-sides to U10 players?

Coach · U6 · Offside · ID: offside-player-explainer-u6

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How do I explain offside to players?”
- Also asked as: “Explain offside simply”
- Also asked as: “What is offside?”
- Also asked as: “How does offside work?”
- Also asked as: “Is offside called?”

U6 does not call offside under PDSA rules.

**You can say:** “We do not call offside in this division.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 11 – Offside, page 14 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 11 — Offside (summary)

## 73. How do I explain off-sides to U10 players?

Coach · U8 · Offside · ID: offside-player-explainer-u8

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How do I explain offside to players?”
- Also asked as: “Explain offside simply”
- Also asked as: “What is offside?”
- Also asked as: “How does offside work?”
- Also asked as: “Is offside called?”

U8 does not call offside under PDSA rules.

**You can say:** “We do not call offside in this division.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 11 – Offside, page 14 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 11 — Offside (summary)

## 74. How do I explain off-sides to U10 players?

Coach · U10 · Offside · ID: offside-player-explainer-u10

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How do I explain offside to players?”
- Also asked as: “Explain offside simply”
- Also asked as: “What is offside?”
- Also asked as: “How does offside work?”
- Also asked as: “Is offside called?”

U10 uses offside. Check the attacker when a teammate plays the ball, not when it arrives. In the opponents’ half, being nearer the goal line than BOTH the ball and the second-last opponent puts a player in an offside position. Level is onside, and hands/arms do not count. Position alone is not a foul: the player must become involved by playing the ball, affecting an opponent or gaining an advantage from a rebound or save. No offside directly from a throw-in, goal kick or corner. An offside offence gives an indirect free kick where it occurs.

**You can say:** “When your teammate plays the ball, stay level with or behind the ball or the second-last opponent. Just standing ahead is not a foul unless you become involved.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 11 – Offside, page 14 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 11 — Offside (summary)

## 78. Describe the different areas of the field?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Field areas · ID: field-areas-explainer

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “What are the different areas of the field?”
- Also asked as: “Explain the field markings”
- Also asked as: “What are the lines and boxes called?”
- Also asked as: “What is the difference between the goal area and penalty area?”

Touchlines (sidelines) are the long boundaries; goal lines are the end boundaries. The halfway line divides the field, with the centre mark and centre circle in the middle. The goal area is the small box in front of goal; the penalty area is the larger box. The penalty mark is inside the larger box, with the penalty arc outside it. Corner arcs mark the corner-kick areas. Lines belong to the areas they enclose. Youth fields can use different sizes and markings. PDSA U6/U8 has a special goal-box rule: players may pass through but must not touch the ball inside it.

**You can say:** “The small box is the goal area. The bigger box is the penalty area. The long lines are sidelines; the end lines are goal lines.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 1 — Field markings (summary)

## 79. When is a goalie restricted from picking up the ball?

Coach · U6 · Goalkeeper · ID: keeper-handling-explainer-u6

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “When can the goalkeeper not pick up the ball?”
- Also asked as: “When is a keeper not allowed to use hands?”
- Also asked as: “Can a keeper pick up a back pass?”
- Also asked as: “Can the goalie pick up a teammates throw in?”

U6 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3. Apply the division’s no-touch goal-box rule and indirect-kick rules instead of giving a player goalkeeper handling privileges.

**You can say:** “There is no goalkeeper in this division.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 80. When is a goalie restricted from picking up the ball?

Coach · U8 · Goalkeeper · ID: keeper-handling-explainer-u8

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “When can the goalkeeper not pick up the ball?”
- Also asked as: “When is a keeper not allowed to use hands?”
- Also asked as: “Can a keeper pick up a back pass?”
- Also asked as: “Can the goalie pick up a teammates throw in?”

U8 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3. Apply the division’s no-touch goal-box rule and indirect-kick rules instead of giving a player goalkeeper handling privileges.

**You can say:** “There is no goalkeeper in this division.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 81. When is a goalie restricted from picking up the ball?

Coach · U10 · Goalkeeper · ID: keeper-handling-explainer-u10

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “When can the goalkeeper not pick up the ball?”
- Also asked as: “When is a keeper not allowed to use hands?”
- Also asked as: “Can a keeper pick up a back pass?”
- Also asked as: “Can the goalie pick up a teammates throw in?”

U10: PDSA awards an indirect free kick for goalkeeper handling after a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in. Do not import an IFAB exception to waive that local rule. Outside their own penalty area, goalkeepers follow ordinary handling restrictions. After releasing hand possession, they cannot handle again until another player touches it. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds gives a corner kick. Ask the coordinator about an uncertain incident using PDSA first.

**You can say:** “Under PDSA rules, you cannot handle a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 85. How do I describe the difference between a yellow card and a red card?

Coach · U6 · Cards · ID: cards-difference-explainer-u6

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “What is the difference between yellow and red cards?”
- Also asked as: “Explain yellow and red cards”
- Also asked as: “What does a yellow card mean?”
- Also asked as: “What does a red card mean?”

PDSA U6 does not award cards. Explain the offence to the player and use the applicable indirect free kick. Get coordinator support for serious misconduct.

**You can say:** “We do not use cards in this division. I will explain what happened and how to restart.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 86. How do I describe the difference between a yellow card and a red card?

Coach · U8 · Cards · ID: cards-difference-explainer-u8

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “What is the difference between yellow and red cards?”
- Also asked as: “Explain yellow and red cards”
- Also asked as: “What does a yellow card mean?”
- Also asked as: “What does a red card mean?”

PDSA U8 does not award cards. Explain the offence to the player and use the applicable indirect free kick. Get coordinator support for serious misconduct.

**You can say:** “We do not use cards in this division. I will explain what happened and how to restart.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 87. How do I describe the difference between a yellow card and a red card?

Coach · U10 · Cards · ID: cards-difference-explainer-u10

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “What is the difference between yellow and red cards?”
- Also asked as: “Explain yellow and red cards”
- Also asked as: “What does a yellow card mean?”
- Also asked as: “What does a red card mean?”

U10: yellow is a formal caution; red is a sending-off. A reckless challenge requires yellow; excessive force or a challenge endangering an opponent requires red. A second yellow in the same match also means a sending-off. A red card can be given without a previous yellow. PDSA provides a one-match suspension for a red or second yellow, subject to review, and does not use temporary dismissals.

**You can say:** “Yellow is a caution. Red means you are sent off. Two yellows in the same game also mean you are sent off.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 91. When do direct kicks get awarded?

Coach · U6 · Free kicks · ID: direct-kick-explainer-u6

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “When is a direct free kick awarded?”
- Also asked as: “What causes a direct kick?”
- Also asked as: “What is a direct free kick?”
- Also asked as: “Which fouls are direct free kicks?”

U6: PDSA makes all fouls indirect free kicks, including handball. Explain the offence to the player. Do not apply the older divisions’ direct-kick rule.

**You can say:** “In this division the free kick is indirect. Another player must touch it before a goal can count.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 92. When do direct kicks get awarded?

Coach · U8 · Free kicks · ID: direct-kick-explainer-u8

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “When is a direct free kick awarded?”
- Also asked as: “What causes a direct kick?”
- Also asked as: “What is a direct free kick?”
- Also asked as: “Which fouls are direct free kicks?”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 93. When do direct kicks get awarded?

Coach · U10 · Free kicks · ID: direct-kick-explainer-u10

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “When is a direct free kick awarded?”
- Also asked as: “What causes a direct kick?”
- Also asked as: “What is a direct free kick?”
- Also asked as: “Which fouls are direct free kicks?”

U10: direct free kicks include careless, reckless or excessively forceful trips, kicks, pushes and challenges, holding an opponent, and handball offences. Not every contact or ball-to-arm touch is an offence. A direct kick may score straight into the opponents’ goal. If a defender commits a direct-kick offence inside their own penalty area while the ball is in play, the restart is a penalty kick. The type of restart and any card are separate decisions.

**You can say:** “That offence is a direct free kick. You can score straight into the other team’s goal.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

Coach · U8 · Free kicks · ID: indirect-kick-explainer-u8

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “What is an indirect free kick?”

## 99. When do in-direct kicks get awarded?

Coach · U10 · Free kicks · ID: indirect-kick-explainer-u10

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “When is an indirect free kick awarded?”
- Also asked as: “What causes an indirect kick?”
- Also asked as: “What is an indirect free kick?”
- Also asked as: “Which fouls are indirect free kicks?”

U10: examples include offside, dangerous play without contact, blocking an opponent’s progress without contact, prohibited goalkeeper handling inside their own penalty area, and many second-touch offences after a restart. This is an examples list, not every case. PDSA also gives an indirect kick for intentional heading. The ball must touch another player before a goal can count; signal an indirect kick with your arm raised. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds is a corner-kick offence, not an indirect kick.

**You can say:** “This is indirect. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count.”

**Supporting sources:** Law 11 – Offside, page 14 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 13 — Free kicks (summary)

## 103. What causes the clock to stop in game?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Match timing · ID: clock-stoppage-explainer

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “When does the clock stop?”
- Also asked as: “Do I stop the clock when the ball goes out?”
- Also asked as: “Does the clock stop for fouls?”
- Also asked as: “Do we add stoppage time?”
- Also asked as: “Do I stop the clock for an injury?”

PDSA says no added time for ordinary stoppages. Its Law 12 also says the clock must not be stopped for injury, regardless of severity. Stop play when safety requires it; the injury clock continues. Approved water breaks and weather suspensions follow their separate league procedures; confirm those arrangements with the Referee Coordinator.

**You can say:** “I am stopping play for safety. Under our rules, the injury clock continues.”

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Appendix B – Weather and Field Condition Policy, page 21 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 104. Ref, why was that a foul?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-1

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Ref, why was that a foul?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: judge the whole challenge. Getting the ball first, an accidental action, or shoulder contact does not automatically make a challenge legal or illegal. Trips, kicks, pushes and charges can be fouls when careless, reckless or excessively forceful; ordinary fair contact is allowed. Describe only the action you actually saw. PDSA makes fouls indirect in this division and requires an explanation to the player; no cards are awarded.

U8: U8: judge the whole challenge. Getting the ball first, an accidental action, or shoulder contact does not automatically make a challenge legal or illegal. Trips, kicks, pushes and charges can be fouls when careless, reckless or excessively forceful; ordinary fair contact is allowed. Describe only the action you actually saw. PDSA makes fouls indirect in this division and requires an explanation to the player; no cards are awarded.

U10: U10: judge the whole challenge. Getting the ball first, an accidental action, or shoulder contact does not automatically make a challenge legal or illegal. Trips, kicks, pushes and charges can be fouls when careless, reckless or excessively forceful; ordinary fair contact is allowed. Describe only the action you actually saw. The restart and any card depend on the offence and location.

U12: U12: judge the whole challenge. Getting the ball first, an accidental action, or shoulder contact does not automatically make a challenge legal or illegal. Trips, kicks, pushes and charges can be fouls when careless, reckless or excessively forceful; ordinary fair contact is allowed. Describe only the action you actually saw. The restart and any card depend on the offence and location.

U14: U14: judge the whole challenge. Getting the ball first, an accidental action, or shoulder contact does not automatically make a challenge legal or illegal. Trips, kicks, pushes and charges can be fouls when careless, reckless or excessively forceful; ordinary fair contact is allowed. Describe only the action you actually saw. The restart and any card depend on the offence and location.

U18: U18: judge the whole challenge. Getting the ball first, an accidental action, or shoulder contact does not automatically make a challenge legal or illegal. Trips, kicks, pushes and charges can be fouls when careless, reckless or excessively forceful; ordinary fair contact is allowed. Describe only the action you actually saw. The restart and any card depend on the offence and location.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 107. The ball hit my hand. Why is that a foul?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-4

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: The ball hit my hand. Why is that a foul?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: not every ball-to-arm touch is handball. Judge deliberate handling and whether the arm makes the body unnaturally bigger without justification from the player’s movement. Accidental contact alone does not settle it. Ask what the arm and ball were doing before judging a particular incident. PDSA uses an indirect kick for handball in this division.

## 109. They pushed me!

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-6

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: They pushed me!”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: judge the whole challenge. Getting the ball first, an accidental action, or shoulder contact does not automatically make a challenge legal or illegal. Trips, kicks, pushes and charges can be fouls when careless, reckless or excessively forceful; ordinary fair contact is allowed. Describe only the action you actually saw. PDSA makes fouls indirect in this division and requires an explanation to the player; no cards are awarded.

U8: U8: judge the whole challenge. Getting the ball first, an accidental action, or shoulder contact does not automatically make a challenge legal or illegal. The restart and any card depend on the offence and location.

## 110. Why didn't you blow your whistle?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-7

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why didn't you blow your whistle?”

The referee can apply advantage when the team offended against benefits from play continuing. If the expected benefit does not develop at that time or within a few seconds, return to the original offence. A missing whistle alone does not prove advantage was applied.

**You can say:** “When you are applying advantage: “Advantage! Keep playing!””

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 111. Can I kick the ball while I'm on the ground?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-8

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can I kick the ball while I'm on the ground?”

Playing the ball while on the ground is not automatically an offence. Judge whether the action threatens injury, prevents a nearby opponent playing for fear of injury, or commits another foul. Contact can change the offence and restart.

**You can say:** “You may play from the ground if you can do it safely.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 113. Why did you stop the game? I'm not hurt!

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-10

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why did you stop the game? I'm not hurt!”

Stop play for a serious injury or immediate safety concern and get the responsible adult or appropriate assistance. The referee judges the safety concern; a player saying they are fine does not require play to continue. A slight injury can usually be addressed when the ball goes out. Confirm the restart from why play stopped.

**You can say:** “I stopped play to make sure you are safe.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 114. The ball touched the line. Isn't it out?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-11

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: The ball touched the line. Isn't it out?”

The whole ball must pass completely over the touchline or goal line, on the ground or in the air, to be out. Touching or overhanging the line is not enough.

**You can say:** “It stays in until the whole ball crosses the line.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** Law 9 – The Ball In and Out of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 115. Why is that their throw-in?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-12

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why is that their throw-in?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6 uses kick-ins instead of throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it onto the field. Wait for another player to touch it before playing it again. Retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: U8: the team opposing the last player to touch the ball gets the throw-in. Take it where the whole ball crossed the touchline. Face the field; part of each foot must be on the line or ground outside it, using both hands from behind and over the head. A foot may be on the line. A throw directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into your own goal gives a corner. PDSA normally allows a teaching retake for an illegal throw unless the specified officials and coaches agree otherwise.

U10: U10: the team opposing the last player to touch the ball gets the throw-in. Take it where the whole ball crossed the touchline. Face the field; part of each foot must be on the line or ground outside it, using both hands from behind and over the head. A foot may be on the line. A throw directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into your own goal gives a corner. PDSA gives this division no throw-in mulligans.

U12: U12: the team opposing the last player to touch the ball gets the throw-in. Take it where the whole ball crossed the touchline. Face the field; part of each foot must be on the line or ground outside it, using both hands from behind and over the head. A foot may be on the line. A throw directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into your own goal gives a corner. PDSA gives this division no throw-in mulligans.

U14: U14: the team opposing the last player to touch the ball gets the throw-in. Take it where the whole ball crossed the touchline. Face the field; part of each foot must be on the line or ground outside it, using both hands from behind and over the head. A foot may be on the line. A throw directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into your own goal gives a corner. PDSA gives this division no throw-in mulligans.

U18: U18: the team opposing the last player to touch the ball gets the throw-in. Take it where the whole ball crossed the touchline. Face the field; part of each foot must be on the line or ground outside it, using both hands from behind and over the head. A foot may be on the line. A throw directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into your own goal gives a corner. PDSA gives this division no throw-in mulligans.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Throw-in; apply the division’s retake and infringement procedure.

**Location:** Ball exit point on the touchline; U6 ball on or outside it.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 15 ? Throw-ins (summary)

## 119. Why is it a corner instead of a goal kick?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-16

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why is it a corner instead of a goal kick?”

When the whole ball crosses the goal line and no goal is scored, last touch by a defender normally gives a corner; last touch by an attacker gives a goal kick. Other special restart offences can also award these restarts.

**You can say:** “When that was the last touch: “Defender touched it last. Corner kick.””

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 17 – The Corner Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 120. Do goal kicks have to leave the penalty area?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-17

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Do goal kicks have to leave the penalty area?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: use PDSA’s special goal-kick procedure: the ball is on the goal line outside the goal box and opponents remain in their own half until it is in play. Do not substitute the older-division penalty-area setup.

U8: U8: use PDSA’s special goal-kick procedure: the ball is on the goal line outside the goal box and opponents remain in their own half until it is in play. Do not substitute the older-division penalty-area setup.

U10: U10: a goal kick does not have to leave the penalty area. It is in play when kicked and clearly moving; opponents normally remain outside the penalty area until then, subject to the law’s quick-restart exceptions.

U12: U12: a goal kick does not have to leave the penalty area. It is in play when kicked and clearly moving; opponents normally remain outside the penalty area until then, subject to the law’s quick-restart exceptions.

U14: U14: a goal kick does not have to leave the penalty area. It is in play when kicked and clearly moving; opponents normally remain outside the penalty area until then, subject to the law’s quick-restart exceptions.

U18: U18: a goal kick does not have to leave the penalty area. It is in play when kicked and clearly moving; opponents normally remain outside the penalty area until then, subject to the law’s quick-restart exceptions.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Goal kick.

**Location:** Within the goal area; apply the goal-kick procedure.

**Discipline:** No automatic card for a legal goal kick.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 16 ? Goal kicks (summary)

## 121. Do I have to wait for the whistle?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-18

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Do I have to wait for the whistle?”

Not every restart needs a whistle. A penalty kick requires the referee’s signal after players are positioned. If the referee has instructed players to wait for the whistle, they must wait. The bundled summaries do not establish a complete whistle checklist for every restart; confirm the referee procedure with your mentor.

**You can say:** “If you have held the restart: “Wait for my whistle.””

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 14 – The Penalty Kick, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 13 — Free kicks (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 14 ? Penalty procedure (summary)

## 122. Can I score straight from a corner kick?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-19

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can I score straight from a corner kick?”

Yes. A corner kick can score directly into the opponents’ goal. Directly into the kicker’s own goal instead gives a corner to the opponents.

**You can say:** “Yes, a corner can go straight into the other team’s goal.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** IFAB 2025/26 Law 17 ? Corners (summary)

## 123. Why did you call that a double touch?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-20

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why did you call that a double touch?”

After many restarts, the taker cannot play the ball again until another player touches it. A second touch normally gives an indirect free kick; handball and some restart-specific cases have different consequences. Identify the restart and what happened before making a ruling.

**You can say:** “Wait for another player to touch the ball before you play it again.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** IFAB 2025/26 Law 13 — Free kicks (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 15 ? Throw-ins (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 16 ? Goal kicks (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 17 ? Corners (summary)

## 124. How was I offside?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-21

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: How was I offside?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: PDSA does not call offside.

U8: U8: PDSA does not call offside.

U10: U10: assess position when a teammate plays or touches the ball, not when the receiver gets it. The attacker must be in the opponents’ half and nearer the goal line than both the ball and second-last opponent. Position alone is not an offence: involvement matters, including interference without touching the ball. There is no offside directly from a throw-in, goal kick or corner. A controlled deliberate play by an opponent can remove the earlier restriction; a deflection or deliberate save does not. A player cannot be in an offside position in their own half, but returning there after being in an offside position at the pass does not erase a later offside offence.

U12: U12: assess position when a teammate plays or touches the ball, not when the receiver gets it. The attacker must be in the opponents’ half and nearer the goal line than both the ball and second-last opponent. Position alone is not an offence: involvement matters, including interference without touching the ball. There is no offside directly from a throw-in, goal kick or corner. A controlled deliberate play by an opponent can remove the earlier restriction; a deflection or deliberate save does not. A player cannot be in an offside position in their own half, but returning there after being in an offside position at the pass does not erase a later offside offence.

U14: U14: assess position when a teammate plays or touches the ball, not when the receiver gets it. The attacker must be in the opponents’ half and nearer the goal line than both the ball and second-last opponent. Position alone is not an offence: involvement matters, including interference without touching the ball. There is no offside directly from a throw-in, goal kick or corner. A controlled deliberate play by an opponent can remove the earlier restriction; a deflection or deliberate save does not. A player cannot be in an offside position in their own half, but returning there after being in an offside position at the pass does not erase a later offside offence.

U18: U18: assess position when a teammate plays or touches the ball, not when the receiver gets it. The attacker must be in the opponents’ half and nearer the goal line than both the ball and second-last opponent. Position alone is not an offence: involvement matters, including interference without touching the ball. There is no offside directly from a throw-in, goal kick or corner. A controlled deliberate play by an opponent can remove the earlier restriction; a deflection or deliberate save does not. A player cannot be in an offside position in their own half, but returning there after being in an offside position at the pass does not erase a later offside offence.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Indirect kick only if an offside offence is established; receiving directly from the listed restarts is exempt.

**Location:** Where the offside offence occurs, if applicable.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 11 – Offside, page 14 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 11 — Offside (summary)
