## 132. Ref, can I make a substitution?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-29

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Ref, can I make a substitution?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: substitutions are unlimited. U6 substitutes may enter anytime under the PDSA summary. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

U8: U8: substitutions are unlimited. Substitute on either team?s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

U10: U10: substitutions are unlimited. Substitute on either team?s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

U12: U12: substitutions are unlimited. Substitute on either team?s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

U14: U14: substitutions are unlimited. Substitute on either team?s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

U18: U18: substitutions are unlimited. Substitute on either team?s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

## 133. Why won't you let my player back in?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-30

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why won't you let my player back in?”

Re-entry depends on why the player left. A player returning after injury must follow the referee’s permission and restart procedure. Bleeding must have stopped and equipment must be clear of blood. Ask why the player left before deciding when they may return.

**You can say:** “Wait for my signal before coming back on.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 134. Can we change goalkeepers?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-31

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can we change goalkeepers?”

A player may change places with the goalkeeper when the referee is informed and the change takes place during a stoppage. Apply the division’s goalkeeper rules.

**You can say:** “Yes, coach. Tell me at a stoppage before changing goalkeepers.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 135. Why wasn't that a penalty?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-32

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why wasn't that a penalty?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: PDSA uses indirect free kicks for fouls; its penalty-kick procedure applies to U10 and older.

U8: U8: PDSA uses indirect free kicks for fouls; its penalty-kick procedure applies to U10 and older.

U10: U10: a defender’s direct-free-kick offence inside their own penalty area while the ball is in play gives a penalty kick. Falling inside the area is not enough if the offence occurred outside. Explain the offence and location you actually judged.

U12: U12: a defender’s direct-free-kick offence inside their own penalty area while the ball is in play gives a penalty kick. Falling inside the area is not enough if the offence occurred outside. Explain the offence and location you actually judged.

U14: U14: a defender’s direct-free-kick offence inside their own penalty area while the ball is in play gives a penalty kick. Falling inside the area is not enough if the offence occurred outside. Explain the offence and location you actually judged.

U18: U18: a defender’s direct-free-kick offence inside their own penalty area while the ball is in play gives a penalty kick. Falling inside the area is not enough if the offence occurred outside. Explain the offence and location you actually judged.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Penalty if the offence and circumstances require it; apply the full procedure.

**Location:** Penalty mark for a penalty kick.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 14 – The Penalty Kick, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 136. Why was that an indirect free kick?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-33

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why was that an indirect free kick?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: all fouls are indirect under PDSA rules, including handball. Explain the offence to the player. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count. Offside is not called in this division.

U8: U8: all fouls are indirect under PDSA rules, including handball. Explain the offence to the player. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count. Offside is not called in this division.

U10: U10: examples include offside, dangerous play without contact, blocking an opponent’s progress without contact, prohibited goalkeeper handling inside their own penalty area, and many second-touch offences after a restart. This is an examples list, not every case. PDSA also gives an indirect kick for intentional heading. The ball must touch another player before a goal can count; signal an indirect kick with your arm raised. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds is a corner-kick offence, not an indirect kick.

U12: U12: examples include offside, dangerous play without contact, blocking an opponent’s progress without contact, prohibited goalkeeper handling inside their own penalty area, and many second-touch offences after a restart. This is an examples list, not every case. PDSA also gives an indirect kick for intentional heading. The ball must touch another player before a goal can count; signal an indirect kick with your arm raised. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds is a corner-kick offence, not an indirect kick.

U14: U14: examples include offside, dangerous play without contact, blocking an opponent’s progress without contact, prohibited goalkeeper handling inside their own penalty area, and many second-touch offences after a restart. This is an examples list, not every case. PDSA also gives an indirect kick for intentional heading. The ball must touch another player before a goal can count; signal an indirect kick with your arm raised. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds is a corner-kick offence, not an indirect kick.

U18: U18: examples include offside, dangerous play without contact, blocking an opponent’s progress without contact, prohibited goalkeeper handling inside their own penalty area, and many second-touch offences after a restart. This is an examples list, not every case. PDSA also gives an indirect kick for intentional heading. The ball must touch another player before a goal can count; signal an indirect kick with your arm raised. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds is a corner-kick offence, not an indirect kick.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Indirect free kick when the cited offence is established.

**Location:** Offence location, subject to Law 13 exceptions and PDSA’s near-goal-box modification.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 13 — Free kicks (summary)

## 137. How much time is left?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-34

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: How much time is left?”

Check the actual match watch and scheduled period before giving a brief estimate. AskRef has no access to this game’s clock; the training guide’s “three minutes” is an example, not the time remaining in your match.

**You can say:** “I will check my watch and tell you the time remaining.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 138. Can you add time for the injury?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-35

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can you add time for the injury?”

PDSA says no added time for ordinary stoppages. Its Law 12 also says the clock must not be stopped for injury, regardless of severity. Stop play when safety requires it; the injury clock continues. Approved water breaks and weather suspensions follow their separate league procedures; confirm those arrangements with the Referee Coordinator.

**You can say:** “I am stopping play for safety. Under our rules, the injury clock continues.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Appendix B – Weather and Field Condition Policy, page 21 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 139. Can you explain that call?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-36

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can you explain that call?”

At a suitable stoppage, explain the specific action and restart briefly and calmly. Describe only what you actually judged; do not invent an observation or argue about every comment. Ask the coordinator for support when needed.

**You can say:** “For a trip you judged: “I called a trip. Free kick. Let’s continue.””

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

## 142. Why did you give my player a yellow card?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-39

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why did you give my player a yellow card?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: PDSA U6 does not award cards. Explain the offence to the player and use the applicable indirect free kick. Get coordinator support for serious misconduct.

U8: PDSA U8 does not award cards. Explain the offence to the player and use the applicable indirect free kick. Get coordinator support for serious misconduct.

U10: U10: yellow is a formal caution; red is a sending-off. A reckless challenge requires yellow; excessive force or a challenge endangering an opponent requires red. A second yellow in the same match also means a sending-off. A red card can be given without a previous yellow. PDSA provides a one-match suspension for a red or second yellow, subject to review, and does not use temporary dismissals.

U12: U12: yellow is a formal caution; red is a sending-off. A reckless challenge requires yellow; excessive force or a challenge endangering an opponent requires red. A second yellow in the same match also means a sending-off. A red card can be given without a previous yellow. PDSA provides a one-match suspension for a red or second yellow, subject to review, and does not use temporary dismissals.

U14: U14: yellow is a formal caution; red is a sending-off. A reckless challenge requires yellow; excessive force or a challenge endangering an opponent requires red. A second yellow in the same match also means a sending-off. A red card can be given without a previous yellow. PDSA provides a one-match suspension for a red or second yellow, subject to review, and does not use temporary dismissals.

U18: U18: yellow is a formal caution; red is a sending-off. A reckless challenge requires yellow; excessive force or a challenge endangering an opponent requires red. A second yellow in the same match also means a sending-off. A red card can be given without a previous yellow. PDSA provides a one-match suspension for a red or second yellow, subject to review, and does not use temporary dismissals.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

## 143. Can you change your decision?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-40

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can you change your decision?”

Consult your assistant and correct an eligible mistake before the restart. A restart decision generally cannot be changed after play restarts, after ending a half and leaving the field, or after abandoning the match. PDSA also makes facts of play and results final once the scorecard is signed. An unsigned card does not allow changing an already-completed restart decision.

**You can say:** “Let me check with my assistant before we restart.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 144. Can I challenge that call?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-41

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can I challenge that call?”

A coach’s disagreement does not require an on-field review. PDSA does not use video or spectator recordings to decide match incidents. Give a brief explanation at an appropriate stoppage and direct performance or result concerns to the Referee Coordinator.

**You can say:** “Coach, please direct further concerns to the Referee Coordinator. Let’s continue.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 6 – The Other Match Officials, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 145. Are you blind? That was a foul!

Parent · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-42

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Are you blind? That was a foul!”

Suggested communication: stay calm, keep the explanation short and do not enter a shouting match or respond personally. Explain only what you saw; never claim a clear view you did not have. If adults become abusive or crowd conduct threatens safety, get the Referee Coordinator or responsible league official’s help. The bundled rules do not establish a complete spectator-sanction procedure.

**You can say:** “Please keep comments respectful. I am asking the Referee Coordinator for support.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: That player is hurt! Stop the game!”

## 154. Can the goalkeeper pick up a pass from a teammate?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-51

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can the goalkeeper pick up a pass from a teammate?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3. Apply the division’s no-touch goal-box rule and indirect-kick rules instead of giving a player goalkeeper handling privileges.

U8: U8 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3. Apply the division’s no-touch goal-box rule and indirect-kick rules instead of giving a player goalkeeper handling privileges.

U10: U10: PDSA awards an indirect free kick for goalkeeper handling after a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in. Do not import an IFAB exception to waive that local rule. Outside their own penalty area, goalkeepers follow ordinary handling restrictions. After releasing hand possession, they cannot handle again until another player touches it. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds gives a corner kick. Ask the coordinator about an uncertain incident using PDSA first.

U12: U12: PDSA awards an indirect free kick for goalkeeper handling after a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in. Do not import an IFAB exception to waive that local rule. Outside their own penalty area, goalkeepers follow ordinary handling restrictions. After releasing hand possession, they cannot handle again until another player touches it. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds gives a corner kick. Ask the coordinator about an uncertain incident using PDSA first.

U14: U14: PDSA awards an indirect free kick for goalkeeper handling after a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in. Do not import an IFAB exception to waive that local rule. Outside their own penalty area, goalkeepers follow ordinary handling restrictions. After releasing hand possession, they cannot handle again until another player touches it. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds gives a corner kick. Ask the coordinator about an uncertain incident using PDSA first.

U18: U18: PDSA awards an indirect free kick for goalkeeper handling after a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in. Do not import an IFAB exception to waive that local rule. Outside their own penalty area, goalkeepers follow ordinary handling restrictions. After releasing hand possession, they cannot handle again until another player touches it. Holding the ball beyond eight seconds gives a corner kick. Ask the coordinator about an uncertain incident using PDSA first.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Prohibited handling inside own area normally gives an indirect kick; holding beyond eight seconds gives a corner. Other cases differ.

**Location:** Offence-specific location, with applicable goal-area exceptions.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

## 157. Why did you call a corner when the goalkeeper held the ball?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-54

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why did you call a corner when the goalkeeper held the ball?”

U6: U6 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3.

U8: U8 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3.

U10: U10: PDSA explicitly penalizes goalkeeper hand/arm control beyond eight seconds with a corner kick. The referee judges the start of control and visibly counts down the final five seconds. This is separate from prohibited pass-back handling, which gives an indirect kick.

U12: U12: PDSA explicitly penalizes goalkeeper hand/arm control beyond eight seconds with a corner kick. The referee judges the start of control and visibly counts down the final five seconds. This is separate from prohibited pass-back handling, which gives an indirect kick.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

## 158. Can players stand in front of the goalkeeper?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-55

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can players stand in front of the goalkeeper?”

An opponent cannot challenge a goalkeeper who controls the ball with the hands or prevent the goalkeeper releasing it. Merely occupying a position is not automatically obstruction; judge whether there is illegal interference. Do not kick or attempt to kick the ball while the keeper is releasing it.

**You can say:** “Do not interfere with the goalkeeper releasing the ball.”

**Restart:** Prohibited handling inside own area normally gives an indirect kick; holding beyond eight seconds gives a corner. Other cases differ.

**Location:** Offence-specific location, with applicable goal-area exceptions.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 159. Does the goalkeeper have to stay on the goal line during a penalty?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-56

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Does the goalkeeper have to stay on the goal line during a penalty?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: PDSA’s penalty-kick procedure applies to U10 and older. This division uses indirect free kicks for fouls.

U8: U8: PDSA’s penalty-kick procedure applies to U10 and older. This division uses indirect free kicks for fouls.

U10: U10: the goalkeeper remains on the goal line until the kick. At the kick, at least part of one foot must touch, be in line with, or be behind the goal line. The kicker may feint during the run-up, but may not fake the kick after completing the run-up. Apply the full penalty procedure for an infringement.

U12: U12: the goalkeeper remains on the goal line until the kick. At the kick, at least part of one foot must touch, be in line with, or be behind the goal line. The kicker may feint during the run-up, but may not fake the kick after completing the run-up. Apply the full penalty procedure for an infringement.

U14: U14: the goalkeeper remains on the goal line until the kick. At the kick, at least part of one foot must touch, be in line with, or be behind the goal line. The kicker may feint during the run-up, but may not fake the kick after completing the run-up. Apply the full penalty procedure for an infringement.

U18: U18: the goalkeeper remains on the goal line until the kick. At the kick, at least part of one foot must touch, be in line with, or be behind the goal line. The kicker may feint during the run-up, but may not fake the kick after completing the run-up. Apply the full penalty procedure for an infringement.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Penalty if the offence and circumstances require it; apply the full procedure.

**Location:** Penalty mark for a penalty kick.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 14 – The Penalty Kick, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 14 ? Penalty procedure (summary)

## 161. Why did we stop for lightning? It's not raining!

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-58

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why did we stop for lightning? It's not raining!”

The bundled PDSA rule identifies an alarming lightning-detection alarm as a suspension condition. Follow the responsible field officials’ shelter and return-to-play directions. The supplied training guide’s 10-mile radius and 30-minute waiting period are not established by this bundled rule text. Do not restart just because an alarm has stopped; confirm the current official weather procedure.

**You can say:** “We are suspended for lightning. Follow the field officials’ shelter directions and wait for clearance to resume.”

**Restart:** Safety suspension or approved break; confirm the continuation procedure with league officials.

**Location:** Record the suspension point, time and score where appropriate.

**Discipline:** No automatic card for a weather or field-condition suspension.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Appendix B – Weather and Field Condition Policy, page 21 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 163. Can we keep playing if the wind is strong?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-60

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can we keep playing if the wind is strong?”

PDSA’s listed weather suspension thresholds include sustained winds of 35 mph or gusts above 50 mph. Follow the responsible PDSA officials’ suspension direction and current field-condition decision.

**You can say:** “We need to follow the league’s wind-safety decision.”

**Restart:** Safety suspension or approved break; confirm the continuation procedure with league officials.

**Location:** Record the suspension point, time and score where appropriate.

## 165. Why can't we play on this wet field?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-62

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why can't we play on this wet field?”

Standing water, snow, or a field condition deemed unsuitable by a PDSA Board Member are listed suspension conditions. Follow the field officials rather than assuming every wet field is playable or every shower requires suspension.

**You can say:** “The field officials have to confirm that the surface is suitable for play.”

**Restart:** Safety suspension or approved break; confirm the continuation procedure with league officials.

**Location:** Record the suspension point, time and score where appropriate.

**Discipline:** No automatic card for a weather or field-condition suspension.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Appendix B – Weather and Field Condition Policy, page 21 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 166. We were winning when the game stopped. Does the score count?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-63

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: We were winning when the game stopped. Does the score count?”

For a weather-suspended PDSA match, at least 50% completed normally counts as a complete match unless coaches and commissioner agree otherwise. Below 50%, it is rescheduled and resumes at the suspension point unless otherwise agreed. Record the time and score and refer the official completion or rescheduling decision to the commissioner.

**You can say:** “I will record the time and score. The commissioner will confirm whether it counts or resumes.”

**Restart:** Safety suspension or approved break; confirm the continuation procedure with league officials.

**Location:** Record the suspension point, time and score where appropriate.

**Discipline:** No automatic card for a weather or field-condition suspension.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Appendix B – Weather and Field Condition Policy, page 21 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 170. Is there offside in our age group?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-67

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Is there offside in our age group?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: PDSA does not call offside.

U8: U8: PDSA does not call offside.

U10: U10: assess position when a teammate plays or touches the ball, not when the receiver gets it. The attacker must be in the opponents’ half and nearer the goal line than both the ball and second-last opponent. Position alone is not an offence: involvement matters, including interference without touching the ball. There is no offside directly from a throw-in, goal kick or corner. A controlled deliberate play by an opponent can remove the earlier restriction; a deflection or deliberate save does not. A player cannot be in an offside position in their own half, but returning there after being in an offside position at the pass does not erase a later offside offence.

U12: U12: assess position when a teammate plays or touches the ball, not when the receiver gets it. The attacker must be in the opponents’ half and nearer the goal line than both the ball and second-last opponent. Position alone is not an offence: involvement matters, including interference without touching the ball. There is no offside directly from a throw-in, goal kick or corner. A controlled deliberate play by an opponent can remove the earlier restriction; a deflection or deliberate save does not. A player cannot be in an offside position in their own half, but returning there after being in an offside position at the pass does not erase a later offside offence.

U14: U14: assess position when a teammate plays or touches the ball, not when the receiver gets it. The attacker must be in the opponents’ half and nearer the goal line than both the ball and second-last opponent. Position alone is not an offence: involvement matters, including interference without touching the ball. There is no offside directly from a throw-in, goal kick or corner. A controlled deliberate play by an opponent can remove the earlier restriction; a deflection or deliberate save does not. A player cannot be in an offside position in their own half, but returning there after being in an offside position at the pass does not erase a later offside offence.

U18: U18: assess position when a teammate plays or touches the ball, not when the receiver gets it. The attacker must be in the opponents’ half and nearer the goal line than both the ball and second-last opponent. Position alone is not an offence: involvement matters, including interference without touching the ball. There is no offside directly from a throw-in, goal kick or corner. A controlled deliberate play by an opponent can remove the earlier restriction; a deflection or deliberate save does not. A player cannot be in an offside position in their own half, but returning there after being in an offside position at the pass does not erase a later offside offence.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Indirect kick only if an offside offence is established; receiving directly from the listed restarts is exempt.

**Location:** Where the offside offence occurs, if applicable.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 11 – Offside, page 14 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 11 — Offside (summary)

## 171. When can we substitute players?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-68

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: When can we substitute players?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6: substitutions are unlimited. U6 substitutes may enter anytime under the PDSA summary. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

U8: U8: substitutions are unlimited. Substitute on either team?s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

U10: U10: substitutions are unlimited. Substitute on either team?s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

U14: U14: substitutions are unlimited. Substitute on either team?s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

U18: U18: substitutions are unlimited. Substitute on either team?s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking that corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may too. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned. Halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee. Each player must receive at least 50% of playing time.

## 172. How long are the halves or quarters?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-69

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: How long are the halves or quarters?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6 plays four 8-minute quarters, with five-minute halftime. Quarter breaks are two minutes. Approved roster or weather adjustments can change the schedule.

U8: U8 plays four 10-minute quarters, with five-minute halftime. Quarter breaks are two minutes. Approved roster or weather adjustments can change the schedule.

U10: U10 plays two 25-minute halves, with five-minute halftime. Approved roster or weather adjustments can change the schedule.

U12: U12 plays two 30-minute halves, with five-minute halftime. Approved roster or weather adjustments can change the schedule.

U14: U14 plays two 30-minute halves, with five-minute halftime. Approved roster or weather adjustments can change the schedule.

U18: U18 plays two 30-minute halves, with five-minute halftime. Approved roster or weather adjustments can change the schedule.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 7 – Duration of the Match, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 173. Can players deliberately head the ball?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-70

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can players deliberately head the ball?”

PDSA’s bundled rule prohibits intentional heading and gives an indirect free kick for it. Explain the offence to the player; do not encourage heading as a way around a handling restriction.

**You can say:** “No intentional heading under our rules. Indirect free kick.”

**Restart:** Indirect free kick when the cited offence is established.

**Location:** Offence location, subject to Law 13 exceptions and PDSA’s near-goal-box modification.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 176. What happens after an incorrect throw-in?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-73

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What happens after an incorrect throw-in?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: U6 uses kick-ins, so the throw-in mulligan rule does not apply.

U8: U8 gets a mulligan (a teaching retake) for an illegal throw-in throughout the season, unless the coaches, commissioner and Referee Coordinator agree otherwise. Explain what to fix before the retake.

U10: U10 has no throw-in mulligans in the PDSA 2025/2026 issue.

U12: U12 has no throw-in mulligans in the PDSA 2025/2026 issue.

U14: U14 has no throw-in mulligans in the PDSA 2025/2026 issue.

U18: U18 has no throw-in mulligans in the PDSA 2025/2026 issue.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Throw-in; apply the division’s retake and infringement procedure.

**Location:** Ball exit point on the touchline; U6 ball on or outside it.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 177. Can a player be sent off?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-74

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player be sent off?”

Ask for the division before answering.

U6: PDSA U6 does not award cards. Explain the offence to the player and use the applicable indirect free kick. Get coordinator support for serious misconduct.

U8: PDSA U8 does not award cards. Explain the offence to the player and use the applicable indirect free kick. Get coordinator support for serious misconduct.

U10: U10: yellow is a formal caution; red is a sending-off. A reckless challenge requires yellow; excessive force or a challenge endangering an opponent requires red. A second yellow in the same match also means a sending-off. A red card can be given without a previous yellow. PDSA provides a one-match suspension for a red or second yellow, subject to review, and does not use temporary dismissals.

U12: U12: yellow is a formal caution; red is a sending-off. A reckless challenge requires yellow; excessive force or a challenge endangering an opponent requires red. A second yellow in the same match also means a sending-off. A red card can be given without a previous yellow. PDSA provides a one-match suspension for a red or second yellow, subject to review, and does not use temporary dismissals.

U14: U14: yellow is a formal caution; red is a sending-off. A reckless challenge requires yellow; excessive force or a challenge endangering an opponent requires red. A second yellow in the same match also means a sending-off. A red card can be given without a previous yellow. PDSA provides a one-match suspension for a red or second yellow, subject to review, and does not use temporary dismissals.

U18: U18: yellow is a formal caution; red is a sending-off. A reckless challenge requires yellow; excessive force or a challenge endangering an opponent requires red. A second yellow in the same match also means a sending-off. A red card can be given without a previous yellow. PDSA provides a one-match suspension for a red or second yellow, subject to review, and does not use temporary dismissals.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

## 178. What happens when one team is winning by a lot?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Young referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-75

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What happens when one team is winning by a lot?”

PDSA’s mercy rule suspends the competitive match when more than half the game is complete and a team leads by more than eight goals. It then becomes a scrimmage with an official. Exactly eight goals ahead or exactly half played does not meet those stated thresholds.

**You can say:** “Once the mercy-rule conditions are met, we follow the scrimmage procedure.”

**Restart:** Depends on why play stopped; an explanation alone does not award a restart.

**Location:** Use the cited procedure after confirming the incident and division.

**Discipline:** Judge the established conduct; the question alone does not establish a card.

**Supporting sources:** Law 10 – Determining the Outcome of a Match, page 13 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 179. The player's back foot came off the ground during the throw. Is that legal?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-76

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “His foot came up”
- Also asked as: “He lifted his back leg”
- Also asked as: “Is that a foul throw?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: At release, part of EACH foot must be on the touchline or on the ground outside it. A foot fully inside without touching the line, or a foot entirely off the ground, fails the requirement. Heels may lift if toes stay grounded correctly; toes may extend into the field if the heel touches the line. PDSA U8 normally gets a teaching retake for an illegal throw throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and coordinator agree otherwise.

U10: At release, part of EACH foot must be on the touchline or on the ground outside it. A foot fully inside without touching the line, or a foot entirely off the ground, fails the requirement. Heels may lift if toes stay grounded correctly; toes may extend into the field if the heel touches the line. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: At release, part of EACH foot must be on the touchline or on the ground outside it. A foot fully inside without touching the line, or a foot entirely off the ground, fails the requirement. Heels may lift if toes stay grounded correctly; toes may extend into the field if the heel touches the line. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: At release, part of EACH foot must be on the touchline or on the ground outside it. A foot fully inside without touching the line, or a foot entirely off the ground, fails the requirement. Heels may lift if toes stay grounded correctly; toes may extend into the field if the heel touches the line. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: At release, part of EACH foot must be on the touchline or on the ground outside it. A foot fully inside without touching the line, or a foot entirely off the ground, fails the requirement. Heels may lift if toes stay grounded correctly; toes may extend into the field if the heel touches the line. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

## 181. Can a player cross their legs while taking a throw-in?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-78

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player cross their legs while taking a throw-in?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U8 normally gets a teaching retake for an illegal throw throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and coordinator agree otherwise.

U10: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

Ask for division first.

U8: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can both feet be on the touchline?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: At release, part of EACH foot must be on the touchline or on the ground outside it. A foot fully inside without touching the line, or a foot entirely off the ground, fails the requirement. Heels may lift if toes stay grounded correctly; toes may extend into the field if the heel touches the line. ## 194. Can a player throw with one hand?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-91

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player throw with one hand?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: Use both hands and throw from behind and over the head. A one-handed delivery or a sideways shoulder throw does not satisfy that procedure. Identify the delivery error rather than calling every unusual technique illegal. PDSA U8 normally gets a teaching retake for an illegal throw throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and coordinator agree otherwise.

U10: Use both hands and throw from behind and over the head. A one-handed delivery or a sideways shoulder throw does not satisfy that procedure. Identify the delivery error rather than calling every unusual technique illegal. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: Use both hands and throw from behind and over the head. A one-handed delivery or a sideways shoulder throw does not satisfy that procedure. Identify the delivery error rather than calling every unusual technique illegal. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: There is no minimum throw distance. A proper throw must enter the field; it can be directed downwards and bounce after entering. Simply dropping it does not meet the throwing requirement. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: There is no minimum throw distance. A proper throw must enter the field; it can be directed downwards and bounce after entering. Simply dropping it does not meet the throwing requirement. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: There is no minimum throw distance. A proper throw must enter the field; it can be directed downwards and bounce after entering. Simply dropping it does not meet the throwing requirement. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

## 199. The ball bounced outside the touchline before entering. What happens?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-96

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: The ball bounced outside the touchline before entering. What happens?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: A properly delivered throw that hits the ground outside before entering, or never enters the field, is retaken by the same team at the same spot. This differs from an incorrectly performed throw that enters play. PDSA U8 normally gets a teaching retake for an illegal throw throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and coordinator agree otherwise.

U10: A properly delivered throw that hits the ground outside before entering, or never enters the field, is retaken by the same team at the same spot. This differs from an incorrectly performed throw that enters play. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: A properly delivered throw that hits the ground outside before entering, or never enters the field, is retaken by the same team at the same spot. This differs from an incorrectly performed throw that enters play. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: A properly delivered throw that hits the ground outside before entering, or never enters the field, is retaken by the same team at the same spot. This differs from an incorrectly performed throw that enters play. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: A properly delivered throw that hits the ground outside before entering, or never enters the field, is retaken by the same team at the same spot. This differs from an incorrectly performed throw that enters play. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Same-team retake for a properly delivered throw that never entered.

**Location:** Original throw-in point.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 15 ? Throw-ins (summary)

## 201. Can a player throw the ball directly into the goal?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-98

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player throw the ball directly into the goal?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: A throw-in cannot score directly. Directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into the thrower’s own goal gives a corner. A touch by another player changes the situation. PDSA U8 normally gets a teaching retake for an illegal throw throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and coordinator agree otherwise.

U10: A throw-in cannot score directly. Directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into the thrower’s own goal gives a corner. A touch by another player changes the situation. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: A throw-in cannot score directly. Directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into the thrower’s own goal gives a corner. A touch by another player changes the situation. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: A throw-in cannot score directly. Directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into the thrower’s own goal gives a corner. A touch by another player changes the situation. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: A throw-in cannot score directly. Directly into the opponents’ goal gives a goal kick; directly into the thrower’s own goal gives a corner. A touch by another player changes the situation. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Goal kick into opponents’ goal; corner into own goal.

**Location:** Applicable goal-kick or corner location for the division.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 15 ? Throw-ins (summary)

## 202. Can a player throw the ball into their goalkeeper's hands?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-99

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player throw the ball into their goalkeeper's hands?”

Ask for division first.

U6 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3. Do not grant a player goalkeeper handling privileges. Apply the division’s field-player and restart rules instead.

U8 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3. Do not grant a player goalkeeper handling privileges. Apply the division’s field-player and restart rules instead.

U10: PDSA awards an indirect free kick for goalkeeper handling after a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in. Do not import an IFAB exception to waive this stated local rule. Outside the penalty area, ordinary handling restrictions apply. Ask the coordinator about an uncertain incident.

U12: PDSA awards an indirect free kick for goalkeeper handling after a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in. Do not import an IFAB exception to waive this stated local rule. Outside the penalty area, ordinary handling restrictions apply. Ask the coordinator about an uncertain incident.

U14: PDSA awards an indirect free kick for goalkeeper handling after a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in. Do not import an IFAB exception to waive this stated local rule. Outside the penalty area, ordinary handling restrictions apply. Ask the coordinator about an uncertain incident.

U18: PDSA awards an indirect free kick for goalkeeper handling after a deliberate teammate pass-back or teammate throw-in. Do not import an IFAB exception to waive this stated local rule. Outside the penalty area, ordinary handling restrictions apply. Ask the coordinator about an uncertain incident.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Indirect free kick for prohibited handling inside own penalty area.

**Location:** Handling location, subject to Law 13 exceptions where PDSA is silent.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 203. Can the player who threw the ball touch it immediately again?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-100

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can the player who threw the ball touch it immediately again?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: After a throw-in enters play, the thrower must wait for another player to touch the ball before touching it again. A normal second touch gives an indirect kick. Handball has separate consequences. PDSA U8 normally gets a teaching retake for an illegal throw throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and coordinator agree otherwise.

U10: After a throw-in enters play, the thrower must wait for another player to touch the ball before touching it again. A normal second touch gives an indirect kick. Handball has separate consequences. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: After a throw-in enters play, the thrower must wait for another player to touch the ball before touching it again. A normal second touch gives an indirect kick. Handball has separate consequences. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: After a throw-in enters play, the thrower must wait for another player to touch the ball before touching it again. A normal second touch gives an indirect kick. Handball has separate consequences. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: After a throw-in enters play, the thrower must wait for another player to touch the ball before touching it again. A normal second touch gives an indirect kick. Handball has separate consequences. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Normally indirect free kick for a second touch; handball differs.

**Location:** Second-touch location, subject to applicable exceptions.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 15 ? Throw-ins (summary)

## 204. Can a defender stand right in front of the thrower?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-101

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a defender stand right in front of the thrower?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: opponents must give the thrower the required 2 metres (2 yards) of space and must not unfairly distract or interfere. PDSA awards no cards in this division; explain the offence and apply the relevant local restart. Get coordinator support for persistent or serious misconduct.

U10: Opponents must be at least 2 metres (2 yards in the law’s yard presentation) from the throw-in point. Unfair distraction or interference, including moving too close, is an offence. Under IFAB it brings a caution and, if the throw was taken, an indirect kick; apply PDSA’s no-card rule in the younger divisions. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: Opponents must be at least 2 metres (2 yards in the law’s yard presentation) from the throw-in point. Unfair distraction or interference, including moving too close, is an offence. Under IFAB it brings a caution and, if the throw was taken, an indirect kick; apply PDSA’s no-card rule in the younger divisions. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: Opponents must be at least 2 metres (2 yards in the law’s yard presentation) from the throw-in point. Unfair distraction or interference, including moving too close, is an offence. Under IFAB it brings a caution and, if the throw was taken, an indirect kick; apply PDSA’s no-card rule in the younger divisions. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: Opponents must be at least 2 metres (2 yards in the law’s yard presentation) from the throw-in point. Unfair distraction or interference, including moving too close, is an offence. Under IFAB it brings a caution and, if the throw was taken, an indirect kick; apply PDSA’s no-card rule in the younger divisions. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** If the throw was taken, indirect free kick for unfair interference.

**Location:** Apply Law 15 and the relevant free-kick location exceptions.

**Discipline:** Caution for unfair distraction/interference where cards apply.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 15 ? Throw-ins (summary)

**Location:** Apply Law 15 and the relevant free-kick location exceptions.

**Discipline:** Caution for unfair distraction/interference where cards apply.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 15 ? Throw-ins (summary)

## 206. Can a player take the throw several yards away from where the ball went out?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-103

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player take the throw several yards away from where the ball went out?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: Take the throw-in from the point where the ball left the field, rather than gaining several yards. Correct the location and apply the division’s procedure if an illegal throw has been taken. PDSA U8 normally gets a teaching retake for an illegal throw throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and coordinator agree otherwise.

U10: Take the throw-in from the point where the ball left the field, rather than gaining several yards. Correct the location and apply the division’s procedure if an illegal throw has been taken. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: Take the throw-in from the point where the ball left the field, rather than gaining several yards. Correct the location and apply the division’s procedure if an illegal throw has been taken. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: Take the throw-in from the point where the ball left the field, rather than gaining several yards. Correct the location and apply the division’s procedure if an illegal throw has been taken. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: Take the throw-in from the point where the ball left the field, rather than gaining several yards. Correct the location and apply the division’s procedure if an illegal throw has been taken. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Throw-in at the correct point.

**Location:** Point where the whole ball crossed the touchline.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 15 ? Throw-ins (summary)

## 207. Can the referee allow play to continue after an illegal throw?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-104

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can the referee allow play to continue after an illegal throw?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: PDSA normally grants a teaching retake for an illegal throw unless coaches, commissioner and coordinator agree otherwise. The general IFAB advantage example must not silently replace that local instruction; confirm an unclear situation with the coordinator.

U10: IFAB’s official FAQ permits advantage after an incorrectly performed throw that enters play if the non-offending team benefits. This is not the rule for a proper throw that never enters the field. Also apply the PDSA division’s teaching-retake procedure. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: IFAB’s official FAQ permits advantage after an incorrectly performed throw that enters play if the non-offending team benefits. This is not the rule for a proper throw that never enters the field. Also apply the PDSA division’s teaching-retake procedure. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: IFAB’s official FAQ permits advantage after an incorrectly performed throw that enters play if the non-offending team benefits. This is not the rule for a proper throw that never enters the field. Also apply the PDSA division’s teaching-retake procedure. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: IFAB’s official FAQ permits advantage after an incorrectly performed throw that enters play if the non-offending team benefits. This is not the rule for a proper throw that never enters the field. Also apply the PDSA division’s teaching-retake procedure. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 15 – The Throw-In, page 17 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 15 ? Throw-ins (summary)

## 208. Can a player do a flip throw-in?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Throw-ins · ID: young-ref-guide-105

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player do a flip throw-in?”

Ask for division first.

U6 uses kick-ins, not throw-ins. Put the ball on or outside the touchline and kick it into play. Another player must touch it before the kicker plays it again. Teaching retakes are unlimited all season; after multiple unsuccessful attempts, the coach should choose another kicker.

U8: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U8 normally gets a teaching retake for an illegal throw throughout the season unless coaches, commissioner and coordinator agree otherwise.

U10: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U10 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U12: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U12 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U14: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U14 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

U18: Crossed, staggered or side-by-side feet, a running approach or a flip are not automatically illegal. Judge the actual release: face the field, part of both feet grounded on the line or outside it, both hands throwing from behind and over the head, at the correct spot. PDSA U18 does not receive throw-in mulligans; an incorrectly performed throw that enters play normally goes to the opponents, subject to advantage.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a defender take a goal kick instead of the goalkeeper?”

Ask for division first.

U6: Any player of the defending team may take a goal kick; it does not have to be the goalkeeper.

U8: Any player of the defending team may take a goal kick; it does not have to be the goalkeeper.

U10: Any player of the defending team may take a goal kick; it does not have to be the goalkeeper.

U12: Any player of the defending team may take a goal kick; it does not have to be the goalkeeper.

U14: Any player of the defending team may take a goal kick; it does not have to be the goalkeeper.

U18: Any player of the defending team may take a goal kick; it does not have to be the goalkeeper.

**Restart:** Goal kick.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.
