## 210. Where can the ball be placed for a goal kick?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-107

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Where can the ball be placed for a goal kick?”

Ask for division first.

U6: place the ball anywhere on the goal line, not in the goal box. Opponents remain in their own half until the ball is in play.

U8: place the ball anywhere on the goal line, not in the goal box. Opponents remain in their own half until the ball is in play.

U10: Place the ball at any point within the goal area.

U12: Place the ball at any point within the goal area.

U14: Place the ball at any point within the goal area.

U18: Place the ball at any point within the goal area.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Goal kick.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 16 ? Goal kicks (summary)

## 211. Does the goal kick have to leave the penalty area?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-108

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Does the goal kick have to leave the penalty area?”

Ask for division first.

U6: PDSA does not require a goal kick to leave the penalty area before a touch. Place it on the goal line outside the goal box. Opponents must remain in their own half until the ball is in play; use this local retreat rule.

U8: PDSA does not require a goal kick to leave the penalty area before a touch. Place it on the goal line outside the goal box. Opponents must remain in their own half until the ball is in play; use this local retreat rule.

U10: A goal kick is in play when kicked and clearly moving; it need not leave the penalty area. A teammate can receive it inside that area. Opponents normally stay outside until it is in play. Quick kicks before opponents can leave have special exceptions; deliberate lingering must not gain an unfair advantage.

U12: A goal kick is in play when kicked and clearly moving; it need not leave the penalty area. A teammate can receive it inside that area. Opponents normally stay outside until it is in play. Quick kicks before opponents can leave have special exceptions; deliberate lingering must not gain an unfair advantage.

U14: A goal kick is in play when kicked and clearly moving; it need not leave the penalty area. A teammate can receive it inside that area. Opponents normally stay outside until it is in play. Quick kicks before opponents can leave have special exceptions; deliberate lingering must not gain an unfair advantage.

U18: A goal kick is in play when kicked and clearly moving; it need not leave the penalty area. A teammate can receive it inside that area. Opponents normally stay outside until it is in play. Quick kicks before opponents can leave have special exceptions; deliberate lingering must not gain an unfair advantage.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Goal kick; retake only when the relevant infringement requires it.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 16 ? Goal kicks (summary)

## 213. Can someone score directly from a goal kick?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-110

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can someone score directly from a goal kick?”

Ask for division first.

U6: A goal kick can score directly into the opponents’ goal. PDSA Law 16 also expressly permits scoring from a goal kick.

U8: A goal kick can score directly into the opponents’ goal. PDSA Law 16 also expressly permits scoring from a goal kick.

U10: A goal kick can score directly into the opponents’ goal. PDSA Law 16 also expressly permits scoring from a goal kick.

U12: A goal kick can score directly into the opponents’ goal. PDSA Law 16 also expressly permits scoring from a goal kick.

U14: A goal kick can score directly into the opponents’ goal. PDSA Law 16 also expressly permits scoring from a goal kick.

U18: A goal kick can score directly into the opponents’ goal. PDSA Law 16 also expressly permits scoring from a goal kick.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Goal followed by kick-off if all scoring conditions are met.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

## 214. What happens if a goal kick goes directly into the kicker's own goal?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-111

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What happens if a goal kick goes directly into the kicker's own goal?”

Ask for division first.

U6: A goal kick directly into the kicker’s own goal gives a corner to the opponents, rather than an own goal.

U8: A goal kick directly into the kicker’s own goal gives a corner to the opponents, rather than an own goal.

U10: A goal kick directly into the kicker’s own goal gives a corner to the opponents, rather than an own goal.

U12: A goal kick directly into the kicker’s own goal gives a corner to the opponents, rather than an own goal.

U14: A goal kick directly into the kicker’s own goal gives a corner to the opponents, rather than an own goal.

U18: A goal kick directly into the kicker’s own goal gives a corner to the opponents, rather than an own goal.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Corner kick to opponents.

**Location:** Applicable corner area.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 16 ? Goal kicks (summary)

## 216. Can the goalkeeper pick up the ball after taking a goal kick?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-113

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can the goalkeeper pick up the ball after taking a goal kick?”

Ask for division first.

U6 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3. Do not grant a player goalkeeper handling privileges. Apply the division’s field-player and restart rules instead.

U8 has no goalkeeper under PDSA Law 3. Do not grant a player goalkeeper handling privileges. Apply the division’s field-player and restart rules instead.

U10: A player taking a goal kick cannot play it again until another player touches it. A goalkeeper handling that second touch inside their own penalty area normally gives an indirect kick; outside it, ordinary handball consequences apply. A second-touch offence stopping a promising attack or denying a clear scoring opportunity can also require discipline.

U12: A player taking a goal kick cannot play it again until another player touches it. A goalkeeper handling that second touch inside their own penalty area normally gives an indirect kick; outside it, ordinary handball consequences apply. A second-touch offence stopping a promising attack or denying a clear scoring opportunity can also require discipline.

U14: A player taking a goal kick cannot play it again until another player touches it. A goalkeeper handling that second touch inside their own penalty area normally gives an indirect kick; outside it, ordinary handball consequences apply. A second-touch offence stopping a promising attack or denying a clear scoring opportunity can also require discipline.

U18: A player taking a goal kick cannot play it again until another player touches it. A goalkeeper handling that second touch inside their own penalty area normally gives an indirect kick; outside it, ordinary handball consequences apply. A second-touch offence stopping a promising attack or denying a clear scoring opportunity can also require discipline.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Normally indirect kick for goalkeeper second-touch handling inside own area.

**Location:** Offence location, subject to Law 13 exceptions.

**Discipline:** May require a card for stopping an attack or denying an obvious scoring opportunity.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 3 – The Players, page 8 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 16 – The Goal Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 16 ? Goal kicks (summary)

## 217. Does the ball have to be completely inside the corner arc?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-114

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Does the ball have to be completely inside the corner arc?”

The whole ball does not need to sit completely inside the corner arc. Part may touch or overhang the boundary of the corner area. The ball must be stationary in the correct corner area.

**You can say:** “Part of the ball may touch or overhang the arc.”

**Restart:** Corner kick.

**Location:** Corner area nearest the exit point.

**Discipline:** No card for a correctly positioned ball.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 17 – The Corner Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 17 ? Corners (summary)

## 218. Can players move the corner flag to make room?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-115

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can players move the corner flag to make room?”

Do not move the corner flag to make room for a kick. PDSA also says referees must not hold or move it during the corner.

**You can say:** “Leave the corner flag in place.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 17 – The Corner Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 17 ? Corners (summary)

## 219. How far away must opponents stand on a corner kick?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-116

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: How far away must opponents stand on a corner kick?”

Opponents stay at least 10 yards from the corner arc until the ball is in play under the adopted IFAB rule. PDSA’s 4/8-yard modification is for free-kick walls; it must not automatically be applied to corners.

**You can say:** “Stay the required distance from the corner arc.”

**Restart:** Corner kick.

**Location:** Distance measured from the corner arc.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 17 – The Corner Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 17 ? Corners (summary)

## 220. Can a corner kick be passed backward?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-117

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a corner kick be passed backward?”

A corner can be passed backwards or sideways. The ball is in play when kicked and clearly moving; it does not need to leave the corner area.

**You can say:** “The corner may go in any direction.”

**Restart:** Corner kick.

**Location:** Correct corner area.

**Discipline:** No card for direction alone.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 17 – The Corner Kick, page 18 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 17 ? Corners (summary)

## 221. Can a player score directly from a free kick?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-118

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player score directly from a free kick?”

Ask for division first.

U6: all fouls give an indirect free kick under PDSA rules, including contact and handball offences. This division does not use the older divisions’ direct-kick or penalty procedure. Explain the offence. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count; the raised arm signals indirect.

U8: all fouls give an indirect free kick under PDSA rules, including contact and handball offences. This division does not use the older divisions’ direct-kick or penalty procedure. Explain the offence. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count; the raised arm signals indirect.

U10: A direct free kick can score straight into the opponents’ goal. An indirect kick needs another player’s touch before a goal can count. The raised arm signals an indirect kick. PDSA’s youngest divisions use indirect kicks for fouls.

U12: A direct free kick can score straight into the opponents’ goal. An indirect kick needs another player’s touch before a goal can count. The raised arm signals an indirect kick. PDSA’s youngest divisions use indirect kicks for fouls.

U14: A direct free kick can score straight into the opponents’ goal. An indirect kick needs another player’s touch before a goal can count. The raised arm signals an indirect kick. PDSA’s youngest divisions use indirect kicks for fouls.

U18: A direct free kick can score straight into the opponents’ goal. An indirect kick needs another player’s touch before a goal can count. The raised arm signals an indirect kick. PDSA’s youngest divisions use indirect kicks for fouls.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 13 — Free kicks (summary)

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why is the referee holding one arm in the air?”

Ask for division first.

U6: all fouls give an indirect free kick under PDSA rules, including contact and handball offences. This division does not use the older divisions’ direct-kick or penalty procedure. Explain the offence. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count; the raised arm signals indirect.

U8: all fouls give an indirect free kick under PDSA rules, including contact and handball offences. This division does not use the older divisions’ direct-kick or penalty procedure. Explain the offence. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count; the raised arm signals indirect.

U10: A direct free kick can score straight into the opponents’ goal. An indirect kick needs another player’s touch before a goal can count. The raised arm signals an indirect kick. PDSA’s youngest divisions use indirect kicks for fouls.

U12: A direct free kick can score straight into the opponents’ goal. An indirect kick needs another player’s touch before a goal can count. The raised arm signals an indirect kick. PDSA’s youngest divisions use indirect kicks for fouls.

U14: A direct free kick can score straight into the opponents’ goal. An indirect kick needs another player’s touch before a goal can count. The raised arm signals an indirect kick. PDSA’s youngest divisions use indirect kicks for fouls.

U18: A direct free kick can score straight into the opponents’ goal. An indirect kick needs another player’s touch before a goal can count. The raised arm signals an indirect kick. PDSA’s youngest divisions use indirect kicks for fouls.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

## 223. How far must defenders stand from a free kick?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-120

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “How far back is the wall?”

Ask for division first.

U6: the PDSA free-kick wall distance is 4 yards from the ball. This overrides the general adult IFAB wall distance. It does not automatically change corner-kick or throw-in distances.

U8: the PDSA free-kick wall distance is 4 yards from the ball. This overrides the general adult IFAB wall distance. It does not automatically change corner-kick or throw-in distances.

U10: the PDSA free-kick wall distance is 8 yards from the ball. This overrides the general adult IFAB wall distance. It does not automatically change corner-kick or throw-in distances.

U12: the PDSA free-kick wall distance is 8 yards from the ball. This overrides the general adult IFAB wall distance. It does not automatically change corner-kick or throw-in distances.

U14: the PDSA free-kick wall distance is 8 yards from the ball. This overrides the general adult IFAB wall distance. It does not automatically change corner-kick or throw-in distances.

U18: the PDSA free-kick wall distance is 8 yards from the ball. This overrides the general adult IFAB wall distance. It does not automatically change corner-kick or throw-in distances.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Free kick.

**Location:** Division-specific wall distance from the ball.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 13 – Free Kicks, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 224. Can a player slide tackle?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-121

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player slide tackle?”

Ask for division first.

U6: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U8: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U10: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator.

U12: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator.

U14: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator.

U18: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the offence, contact and location; apply the local division rule.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** Careless: normally no card. Reckless: caution. Excessive force/endangering safety: sending-off where cards apply.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 226. Can a player shield the ball using their body?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-123

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player shield the ball using their body?”

Ask for division first.

U6: A player may shield a ball within playing distance by placing their body between it and an opponent. They may not hold the opponent off with their arms or body. Being in a position is different from moving to obstruct a player when the ball is not within playing distance. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U8: A player may shield a ball within playing distance by placing their body between it and an opponent. They may not hold the opponent off with their arms or body. Being in a position is different from moving to obstruct a player when the ball is not within playing distance. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U10: A player may shield a ball within playing distance by placing their body between it and an opponent. They may not hold the opponent off with their arms or body. Being in a position is different from moving to obstruct a player when the ball is not within playing distance.

U12: A player may shield a ball within playing distance by placing their body between it and an opponent. They may not hold the opponent off with their arms or body. Being in a position is different from moving to obstruct a player when the ball is not within playing distance.

U14: A player may shield a ball within playing distance by placing their body between it and an opponent. They may not hold the opponent off with their arms or body. Being in a position is different from moving to obstruct a player when the ball is not within playing distance.

U18: A player may shield a ball within playing distance by placing their body between it and an opponent. They may not hold the opponent off with their arms or body. Being in a position is different from moving to obstruct a player when the ball is not within playing distance.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

## 230. Can a player kick the ball above their head?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-127

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player kick the ball above their head?”

Ask for division first.

U6: A high or bicycle kick, falling on the ball or trapping it between the legs is not automatically an offence. Judge whether the action threatens injury or prevents a nearby opponent playing through fear of injury. Dangerous play without contact normally gives an indirect kick; contact can produce a different foul. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U8: A high or bicycle kick, falling on the ball or trapping it between the legs is not automatically an offence. Judge whether the action threatens injury or prevents a nearby opponent playing through fear of injury. Dangerous play without contact normally gives an indirect kick; contact can produce a different foul. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U10: A high or bicycle kick, falling on the ball or trapping it between the legs is not automatically an offence. Judge whether the action threatens injury or prevents a nearby opponent playing through fear of injury. Dangerous play without contact normally gives an indirect kick; contact can produce a different foul.

U12: A high or bicycle kick, falling on the ball or trapping it between the legs is not automatically an offence. Judge whether the action threatens injury or prevents a nearby opponent playing through fear of injury. Dangerous play without contact normally gives an indirect kick; contact can produce a different foul.

U14: A high or bicycle kick, falling on the ball or trapping it between the legs is not automatically an offence. Judge whether the action threatens injury or prevents a nearby opponent playing through fear of injury. Dangerous play without contact normally gives an indirect kick; contact can produce a different foul.

U18: A high or bicycle kick, falling on the ball or trapping it between the legs is not automatically an offence. Judge whether the action threatens injury or prevents a nearby opponent playing through fear of injury. Dangerous play without contact normally gives an indirect kick; contact can produce a different foul.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Indirect kick for dangerous play without contact; contact offences can differ.

**Location:** Offence location, subject to applicable exceptions.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 232. Can a player use their knee to control the ball?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-129

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player use their knee to control the ball?”

Using the knee, chest or stomach to control the ball is normally legal. Judge any separate dangerous play or other offence; these body parts are not hand/arm contact.

**You can say:** “Knee, chest and stomach control are allowed if the play is safe.”

**Restart:** Play continues if no separate offence.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No card for legal control.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 235. Can a player trap the ball between their legs?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-132

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player trap the ball between their legs?”

Ask for division first.

U6: A high or bicycle kick, falling on the ball or trapping it between the legs is not automatically an offence. Judge whether the action threatens injury or prevents a nearby opponent playing through fear of injury. Dangerous play without contact normally gives an indirect kick; contact can produce a different foul. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

Explain the offence to the player.

Dangerous play without contact normally gives an indirect kick; contact can produce a different foul.

## 236. What if a player kicks another player's foot while trying to kick the ball?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-133

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if a player kicks another player's foot while trying to kick the ball?”

Ask for division first.

U6: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U8: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U10: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator.

U12: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator.

U14: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator.

U18: Judge the action and its safety, not just who got the ball first or whether someone fell. A slide, collision or arm position is not automatically a foul. Careless, reckless or excessively forceful kicks, trips, pushes and challenges, holding or unfair impeding can be offences. Confirm any additional local slide-tackle restrictions with the coordinator.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the offence, contact and location; apply the local division rule.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** Careless: normally no card. Reckless: caution. Excessive force/endangering safety: sending-off where cards apply.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 238. Can the referee call a foul against both players?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-135

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can the referee call a foul against both players?”

Ask for division first.

U6: Identify whether offences happened together or one after another. For simultaneous offences the referee punishes the more serious one, considering sanction, restart, physical severity and tactical impact. Do not invent two conflicting restarts. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U8: Identify whether offences happened together or one after another. For simultaneous offences the referee punishes the more serious one, considering sanction, restart, physical severity and tactical impact. Do not invent two conflicting restarts. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U10: Identify whether offences happened together or one after another. For simultaneous offences the referee punishes the more serious one, considering sanction, restart, physical severity and tactical impact. Do not invent two conflicting restarts.

U12: Identify whether offences happened together or one after another. For simultaneous offences the referee punishes the more serious one, considering sanction, restart, physical severity and tactical impact. Do not invent two conflicting restarts.

U14: Identify whether offences happened together or one after another. For simultaneous offences the referee punishes the more serious one, considering sanction, restart, physical severity and tactical impact. Do not invent two conflicting restarts.

U18: Identify whether offences happened together or one after another. For simultaneous offences the referee punishes the more serious one, considering sanction, restart, physical severity and tactical impact. Do not invent two conflicting restarts.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** Depends on each established offence.

## 239. What if a player deliberately blocks an opponent who doesn't have the ball?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-136

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if a player deliberately blocks an opponent who doesn't have the ball?”

Ask for division first.

U6: all fouls give an indirect free kick under PDSA rules, including contact and handball offences. This division does not use the older divisions’ direct-kick or penalty procedure. Explain the offence. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count; the raised arm signals indirect.

U8: all fouls give an indirect free kick under PDSA rules, including contact and handball offences. This division does not use the older divisions’ direct-kick or penalty procedure. Explain the offence. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count; the raised arm signals indirect.

U10: Moving into an opponent’s path to block progress when the ball is not within playing distance can be impeding. Without contact it normally gives an indirect kick; with contact it can give a direct kick. Merely occupying an existing position is different.

U12: Moving into an opponent’s path to block progress when the ball is not within playing distance can be impeding. Without contact it normally gives an indirect kick; with contact it can give a direct kick. Merely occupying an existing position is different.

U14: Moving into an opponent’s path to block progress when the ball is not within playing distance can be impeding. Without contact it normally gives an indirect kick; with contact it can give a direct kick. Merely occupying an existing position is different.

U18: Moving into an opponent’s path to block progress when the ball is not within playing distance can be impeding. Without contact it normally gives an indirect kick; with contact it can give a direct kick. Merely occupying an existing position is different.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Indirect without contact; direct with contact, subject to PDSA division modifications.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 240. What happens if someone spits at another player?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-137

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What happens if someone spits at another player?”

Ask for division first.

U6: PDSA does not award cards. Explain the offence and apply the applicable indirect kick. Stop unsafe conduct and get a responsible league official’s help for serious misconduct; do not leave a young referee to handle a threatening situation alone.

U8: PDSA does not award cards. Explain the offence and apply the applicable indirect kick. Stop unsafe conduct and get a responsible league official’s help for serious misconduct; do not leave a young referee to handle a threatening situation alone.

U10: Spitting at another person is a sending-off offence under adopted IFAB rules where cards apply. Get coordinator support and protect the young referee’s safety. PDSA’s youngest divisions do not award cards, so involve the responsible league official for serious misconduct.

U12: Spitting at another person is a sending-off offence under adopted IFAB rules where cards apply. Get coordinator support and protect the young referee’s safety. PDSA’s youngest divisions do not award cards, so involve the responsible league official for serious misconduct.

U14: Spitting at another person is a sending-off offence under adopted IFAB rules where cards apply. Get coordinator support and protect the young referee’s safety. PDSA’s youngest divisions do not award cards, so involve the responsible league official for serious misconduct.

U18: Spitting at another person is a sending-off offence under adopted IFAB rules where cards apply. Get coordinator support and protect the young referee’s safety. PDSA’s youngest divisions do not award cards, so involve the responsible league official for serious misconduct.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** Sending-off for spitting where cards apply; coordinator support in no-card divisions.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 244. Can a player enter the field without the referee's permission?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-141

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player enter the field without the referee's permission?”

Ask for division first.

U6 substitutions may be made anytime under PDSA’s modified procedure.

U8: substitutions are allowed on either team’s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or on your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking a corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may also substitute. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned; halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee.

U10: substitutions are allowed on either team’s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or on your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking a corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may also substitute. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned; halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee.

U12: substitutions are allowed on either team’s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or on your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking a corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may also substitute. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned; halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee.

U14: substitutions are allowed on either team’s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or on your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking a corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may also substitute. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned; halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee.

U18: substitutions are allowed on either team’s goal kick, goal, caution or injury, or on your own corner/throw-in. If the team taking a corner or throw-in substitutes, the opponents may also substitute. Wait at midfield and enter when summoned; halftime substitutions do not require alerting the referee.

## 246. Can players switch positions during play?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-143

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can players switch positions during play?”

Outfield players may switch playing positions without a substitution. A goalkeeper change requires informing the referee and a stoppage. Apply the approved team format and division’s goalkeeper rules.

**You can say:** “Outfield positions may change; tell me before a goalkeeper change at a stoppage.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

## 247. Can a player play without shin guards?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-144

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player play without shin guards?”

Shin guards are required and must provide suitable protection, covered by socks. Correct missing equipment before allowing participation under the equipment procedure.

**You can say:** “You need suitable shin guards under your socks.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** Missing equipment is not automatically a card; refusal or other conduct can change the sanction.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

## 248. Can a player wear earrings if they're covered with tape?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-145

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Are taped earrings allowed?”
- Also asked as: “Can she play with taped stud earrings?”

PDSA Law 4 permits small stud earrings covered with tape or equivalent. Other jewellery exceptions require Referee Coordinator or Board approval. Do not replace that local provision with a blanket IFAB prohibition. Refer unclear equipment or safety concerns to those officials; tape is not general permission for all jewellery.

**You can say:** “PDSA permits covered small studs. Let me confirm any other equipment or safety concern.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** Confirm any required equipment approval; no automatic card from this question alone.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

## 250. Can a player wear a medical bracelet?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-147

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player wear a medical bracelet?”

Do not assume a medical bracelet is automatically exempt. PDSA’s jewellery and approval rules still need to be followed. Ask the Referee Coordinator or Board Member to confirm the permitted safe arrangement.

**You can say:** “Let me get the coordinator’s equipment decision.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 4 – The Players’ Equipment, page 9 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 4 ? Equipment safety (summary)

## 251. What if a player's shoe comes off during play?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-148

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if a player's shoe comes off during play?”

A player who accidentally loses footwear must replace it as soon as possible and no later than the next stoppage. An immediate play or goal before correcting it can remain valid. Stop if there is a separate safety reason; deliberate play without required footwear is a different situation.

**You can say:** “Put your shoe back on as soon as possible, no later than the next stoppage.”

**Restart:** Play need not stop solely for accidentally lost footwear.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card for accidental footwear loss.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 4 – The Players’ Equipment, page 9 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 4 ? Equipment safety (summary)

## 252. Can a player wear a baseball cap?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-149

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player wear a baseball cap?”

IFAB permits a safe goalkeeper’s cap. Other headgear must meet its safety requirements; this is not blanket permission for every player to wear a baseball cap. Apply PDSA equipment decisions.

**You can say:** “A safe goalkeeper’s cap may be allowed; let me check the equipment.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 4 – The Players’ Equipment, page 9 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 4 ? Equipment safety (summary)

## 253. Can a player wear a cast or brace?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-150

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player wear a cast or brace?”

PDSA requires hard casts, braces or exposed hard material to be approved by a Board Member, Commissioner or Referee Coordinator, with an approved protective padding layer. The referee should confirm that approval and safety.

**You can say:** “We need the required approval and protective padding.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 4 – The Players’ Equipment, page 9 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026)

## 254. What if the ball hits the referee?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-151

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if the ball hits the referee?”

Referee contact does not always stop play. If the ball remains on the field and a promising attack, direct goal or possession change results, stop and use a dropped ball. Otherwise play normally continues; if it leaves the field, decide the exit restart from the last player touch.

**You can say:** “What happened after the contact determines the restart.”

**Restart:** Dropped ball only for the listed referee-contact outcomes.

**Location:** Recipient and location depend on the adopted Law 8 circumstances.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 9 – The Ball In and Out of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 8 ? Dropped-ball procedure (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 9 ? Ball status (summary)

## 255. What if the ball hits the goalpost and comes back?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-152

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if the ball hits the goalpost and comes back?”

A rebound from a goalpost, crossbar or corner flag stays in play if the whole ball remains within the field and no separate reason has stopped play.

**You can say:** “Still in the field: play on.”

**Restart:** Play continues unless another reason requires stopping.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No card for the rebound itself.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** IFAB 2025/26 Law 9 ? Ball status (summary)

## 256. What if the ball hits the corner flag and stays in?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-153

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if the ball hits the corner flag and stays in?”

A rebound from a goalpost, crossbar or corner flag stays in play if the whole ball remains within the field and no separate reason has stopped play.

**You can say:** “Still in the field: play on.”

**Restart:** Play continues unless another reason requires stopping.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No card for the rebound itself.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

## 257. What if a second ball rolls onto the field?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-154

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if a second ball rolls onto the field?”

An extra ball or spectator does not automatically require stopping if there is no interference. Stop for interference or a safety threat and get responsible league help when needed. Determine the proper restart; a ball already entering goal has specific exceptions, so do not automatically erase every goal.

**You can say:** “Keep clear of the field. I am getting league help if needed.”

**Restart:** Depends on interference and whether play was already stopped.

**Location:** Confirm the applicable Law 8 procedure; do not guess.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 8 ? Dropped-ball procedure (summary)

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-155

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if a spectator walks onto the field?”

Determine the proper restart; a ball already entering goal has specific exceptions, so do not automatically erase every goal.

## 259. What if the goal falls over or becomes unsafe?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-156

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if the goal falls over or becomes unsafe?”

Stop play for an unsafe or fallen goal, keep players clear and get responsible adults to secure or repair it. Do not resume until safe. IFAB requires goals to be firmly secured; a broken crossbar that cannot be repaired requires abandonment.

**You can say:** “Stop. Stay away from the goal until it is made safe.”

**Restart:** Establish why play stopped and the applicable repair/restart procedure.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 1 — Field markings (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 260. Can a player leave the field to get around a defender?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-157

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player leave the field to get around a defender?”

Temporarily leaving the field as part of a playing movement is not automatically an offence. Deliberately leaving or re-entering to gain an unfair advantage, avoid offside or evade the referee’s permission procedure can be a different matter.

**You can say:** “A temporary playing movement off the field is not automatically an offence.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** IFAB 2025/26 Law 11 — Offside (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 3 ? Player entry (summary)

## 261. Can a player deliberately kick the ball out so someone can receive treatment?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-158

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a player deliberately kick the ball out so someone can receive treatment?”

A player can put the ball out to allow attention to an injury. Stop yourself for a serious injury rather than waiting for this courtesy. If the ball goes out first, use the normal exit restart; do not invent a special compulsory give-back restart.

**You can say:** “We will use the restart for how the ball left play.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 9 – The Ball In and Out of Play, page 12 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 262. What if a team refuses to continue playing?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-159

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if a team refuses to continue playing?”

Do not force a team to participate or argue with the coach. Involve the Referee Coordinator or responsible PDSA official, record the facts and follow the league’s suspension or abandonment procedure. Do not invent a forfeit score.

**You can say:** “I am contacting the league official to confirm the procedure.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 263. What if a coach comes onto the field without permission?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-160

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if a coach comes onto the field without permission?”

Ask for division first.

PDSA permits one U6 coach on the field throughout play. That exception does not permit interference, threats or unsafe conduct; get league support when needed.

U8: Establish why the coach entered and whether they interfered or threatened safety. PDSA expressly permits one U6 coach on the field. Other entries must follow the relevant permission and team-official procedure; get coordinator support rather than assuming every entry has the same sanction.

U10: Establish why the coach entered and whether they interfered or threatened safety. PDSA expressly permits one U6 coach on the field. Other entries must follow the relevant permission and team-official procedure; get coordinator support rather than assuming every entry has the same sanction.

U12: Establish why the coach entered and whether they interfered or threatened safety. PDSA expressly permits one U6 coach on the field. Other entries must follow the relevant permission and team-official procedure; get coordinator support rather than assuming every entry has the same sanction.

U14: Establish why the coach entered and whether they interfered or threatened safety. PDSA expressly permits one U6 coach on the field. Other entries must follow the relevant permission and team-official procedure; get coordinator support rather than assuming every entry has the same sanction.

U18: Establish why the coach entered and whether they interfered or threatened safety. PDSA expressly permits one U6 coach on the field. Other entries must follow the relevant permission and team-official procedure; get coordinator support rather than assuming every entry has the same sanction.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

## 264. Can the referee change a call after the whistle?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-161

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can the referee change a call after the whistle?”

A whistle does not make every decision unchangeable. An eligible mistake can be corrected before play restarts, within Law 5’s limits. Restart decisions generally cannot be changed after restarting, after ending a half and leaving the field, or after abandoning. PDSA’s signed-scorecard finality does not override these earlier limits.

**You can say:** “Let me check with my assistant before the restart.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 265. What if the referee accidentally blows the whistle?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-162

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if the referee accidentally blows the whistle?”

An accidental whistle stops play. If there was no other applicable restart reason, a dropped ball is normally needed. Establish whether the ball was already out or another offence had already determined the restart; do not assume a generic dropped-ball recipient or location.

**You can say:** “I stopped play accidentally. Let me confirm the correct restart.”

**Restart:** Normally dropped ball if play stopped only for the accidental whistle.

**Location:** Apply the adopted Law 8 recipient and location procedure.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 8 ? Dropped-ball procedure (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 9 ? Ball status (summary)

## 266. What if the game ball becomes flat?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-163

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if the game ball becomes flat?”

Replace a defective ball with the referee’s permission. If it becomes defective during open play, stop and restart with a dropped ball. If it becomes defective at a restart, that restart is retaken; a penalty becoming defective in flight has a specific retake rule.

**You can say:** “We need a sound ball. I will confirm the restart from when it became defective.”

**Restart:** Dropped ball during open play; original restart retaken when applicable.

**Location:** Depends on when and where the defect occurred.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** IFAB 2025/26 Law 2 ? Defective ball (summary); IFAB 2025/26 Law 8 ? Dropped-ball procedure (summary)

## 267. What if the referee didn't see who touched the ball last?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-164

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: What if the referee didn't see who touched the ball last?”

Consult the assistant if available and use the information actually observed. Do not claim certainty, invent a last touch or invent a coin-toss procedure. Seek an experienced official’s help if still unsure about the applicable rule or procedure.

**You can say:** “Let me check with my assistant.”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 5 – The Referee, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 6 – The Other Match Officials, page 10 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 5 ? Referee authority and safety (summary)

## 268. Can a referee give a yellow card to a coach?

Player · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-165

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can a referee give a yellow card to a coach?”

Ask for division first.

U6: PDSA does not award cards. Explain the offence and apply the applicable indirect kick. Stop unsafe conduct and get a responsible league official’s help for serious misconduct; do not leave a young referee to handle a threatening situation alone.

U8: PDSA does not award cards. Explain the offence and apply the applicable indirect kick. Stop unsafe conduct and get a responsible league official’s help for serious misconduct; do not leave a young referee to handle a threatening situation alone.

U10: Where cards apply, a coach can receive a yellow for qualifying misconduct, and a red can be given directly for a sending-off offence without a previous yellow. Judge the actual conduct and applicable team-official provisions. Apply PDSA’s division modifications and get support for serious misconduct.

U12: Where cards apply, a coach can receive a yellow for qualifying misconduct, and a red can be given directly for a sending-off offence without a previous yellow. Judge the actual conduct and applicable team-official provisions. Apply PDSA’s division modifications and get support for serious misconduct.

U14: Where cards apply, a coach can receive a yellow for qualifying misconduct, and a red can be given directly for a sending-off offence without a previous yellow. Judge the actual conduct and applicable team-official provisions. Apply PDSA’s division modifications and get support for serious misconduct.

U18: Where cards apply, a coach can receive a yellow for qualifying misconduct, and a red can be given directly for a sending-off offence without a previous yellow. Judge the actual conduct and applicable team-official provisions. Apply PDSA’s division modifications and get support for serious misconduct.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Depends on the actual incident; no automatic restart from the question alone.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

## 269. Can you penalize my player when the ball is already out?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-166

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Can you penalize my player when the ball is already out?”

Ask for division first.

U6: PDSA does not award cards. Explain the offence and apply the applicable indirect kick. Stop unsafe conduct and get a responsible league official’s help for serious misconduct; do not leave a young referee to handle a threatening situation alone.

U8: PDSA does not award cards. Explain the offence and apply the applicable indirect kick. Stop unsafe conduct and get a responsible league official’s help for serious misconduct; do not leave a young referee to handle a threatening situation alone.

U10: Misconduct can be dealt with while the ball is out. The restart normally stays the one already required; a later off-the-ball incident does not turn a prior exit into a penalty kick. Apply the appropriate division’s discipline rules.

U12: Misconduct can be dealt with while the ball is out. The restart normally stays the one already required; a later off-the-ball incident does not turn a prior exit into a penalty kick. Apply the appropriate division’s discipline rules.

U14: Misconduct can be dealt with while the ball is out. The restart normally stays the one already required; a later off-the-ball incident does not turn a prior exit into a penalty kick. Apply the appropriate division’s discipline rules.

U18: Misconduct can be dealt with while the ball is out. The restart normally stays the one already required; a later off-the-ball incident does not turn a prior exit into a penalty kick. Apply the appropriate division’s discipline rules.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Normally retain the restart already required when the ball went out.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)

## 270. Why didn't you call that foul against the goalkeeper?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-167

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Why didn't you call that foul against the goalkeeper?”

Ask for division first.

U6: Goalkeepers can commit ordinary fouls. Their protection while controlling the ball with the hands does not excuse an illegal challenge. Ask what action and location were observed before deciding a particular case. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U8: Goalkeepers can commit ordinary fouls. Their protection while controlling the ball with the hands does not excuse an illegal challenge. Ask what action and location were observed before deciding a particular case. Apply PDSA’s modification: all fouls are indirect and no cards are awarded in this division. Explain the offence to the player.

U10: Goalkeepers can commit ordinary fouls. Their protection while controlling the ball with the hands does not excuse an illegal challenge. Ask what action and location were observed before deciding a particular case.

U12: Goalkeepers can commit ordinary fouls. Their protection while controlling the ball with the hands does not excuse an illegal challenge. Ask what action and location were observed before deciding a particular case.

U14: Goalkeepers can commit ordinary fouls. Their protection while controlling the ball with the hands does not excuse an illegal challenge. Ask what action and location were observed before deciding a particular case.

U18: Goalkeepers can commit ordinary fouls. Their protection while controlling the ball with the hands does not excuse an illegal challenge. Ask what action and location were observed before deciding a particular case.

## 271. Is every handball in the penalty area a penalty?

Coach · U6/U8/U10/U12/U14/U18 · Expanded referee training · ID: young-ref-guide-168

Status: Draft — coordinator review pending

- Also asked as: “Please explain: Is every handball in the penalty area a penalty?”

Ask for division first.

U6: all fouls give an indirect free kick under PDSA rules, including contact and handball offences. This division does not use the older divisions’ direct-kick or penalty procedure. Explain the offence. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count; the raised arm signals indirect.

U8: all fouls give an indirect free kick under PDSA rules, including contact and handball offences. This division does not use the older divisions’ direct-kick or penalty procedure. Explain the offence. Another player must touch the ball before a goal can count; the raised arm signals indirect.

U10: First establish an actual offence and its location while the ball is in play. Not every ball-to-arm contact is handball. A defender’s direct-kick offence inside their own penalty area gives a penalty; an offence outside does not become a penalty because a player then falls inside. Goalkeeper handling offences inside their own area are distinct.

U12: First establish an actual offence and its location while the ball is in play. Not every ball-to-arm contact is handball. A defender’s direct-kick offence inside their own penalty area gives a penalty; an offence outside does not become a penalty because a player then falls inside. Goalkeeper handling offences inside their own area are distinct.

U14: First establish an actual offence and its location while the ball is in play. Not every ball-to-arm contact is handball. A defender’s direct-kick offence inside their own penalty area gives a penalty; an offence outside does not become a penalty because a player then falls inside. Goalkeeper handling offences inside their own area are distinct.

U18: First establish an actual offence and its location while the ball is in play. Not every ball-to-arm contact is handball. A defender’s direct-kick offence inside their own penalty area gives a penalty; an offence outside does not become a penalty because a player then falls inside. Goalkeeper handling offences inside their own area are distinct.

**You can say:** “Which division is this game?”

**Restart:** Direct or penalty kick only if the offence and location require it; younger PDSA divisions differ.

**Location:** Use the applicable restart location after establishing the incident.

**Discipline:** No automatic card; judge the actual conduct and division.

**Help needed:** Confirm missing facts, full procedure or coordinator direction as described above.

**Supporting sources:** Law 12 – Fouls and Misconduct, pages 14–15 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); Law 14 – The Penalty Kick, page 16 · PDSA 2025/2026 Issue 09 (02/2026); IFAB 2025/26 Law 12 — Fouls, cards and goalkeepers (summary)
